```
1→"""I/O and transactional persistence helpers for plan override commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
     9→from desloppify.engine.plan import get_plan_file, plan_path_for_state
    10→
    11→
    12→def _resolve_state_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    13→    return path if path is not None else state_mod.get_state_file()
    14→
    15→
    16→def _resolve_plan_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    17→    return path if path is not None else get_plan_file()
    18→
    19→
    20→def _plan_file_for_state(state_file: Path | None) -> Path | None:
    21→    if state_file is None:
    22→        return None
    23→    return plan_path_for_state(state_file)
    24→
    25→
    26→def _snapshot_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    27→    if not path.exists():
    28→        return None
    29→    return path.read_text()
    30→
    31→
    32→def _restore_file_snapshot(path: Path, snapshot: str | None) -> None:
    33→    if snapshot is None:
    34→        try:
    35→            path.unlink()
    36→        except FileNotFoundError:
    37→            return
    38→        return
    39→    safe_write_text(path, snapshot)
    40→
    41→
    42→def save_plan_state_transactional(
    43→    *,
    44→    plan: dict,
    45→    plan_path: Path | None,
    46→    state_data: dict,
    47→    state_path_value: Path | None,
    48→) -> None:
    49→    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
    50→    effective_plan_path = _resolve_plan_file(plan_path)
    51→    effective_state_path = _resolve_state_file(state_path_value)
    52→    plan_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_plan_path)
    53→    state_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_state_path)
    54→
    55→    try:
    56→        state_mod.save_state(state_data, effective_state_path)
    57→        # Keep this import-time indirection so tests monkeypatching
    58→        # override_handlers.save_plan still affect transactional writes.
    59→        from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    60→
    61→        host.save_plan(plan, effective_plan_path)
    62→    except Exception:
    63→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_state_path, state_snapshot)
    64→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_plan_path, plan_snapshot)
    65→        raise
    66→
    67→
    68→__all__ = [
    69→    "_plan_file_for_state",
    70→    "save_plan_state_transactional",
    71→]
    72→
```

```
1→"""Describe/note/reopen/focus/scan-gate handlers for plan overrides."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     9→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    10→
    11→
    12→def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    13→    """Set augmented description on issues."""
    14→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    15→
    16→    state = host.command_runtime(args).state
    17→    if not host.require_completed_scan(state):
    18→        return
    19→
    20→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    21→    text: str = getattr(args, "text", "")
    22→
    23→    plan = host.load_plan()
    24→    issue_ids = host.resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    25→    if not issue_ids:
    26→        print(colorize("  No matching issues found.", "yellow"))
    27→        return
    28→
    29→    for fid in issue_ids:
    30→        host.describe_issue(plan, fid, text or None)
    31→    host.append_log_entry(plan, "describe", issue_ids=issue_ids, actor="user", detail={"text": text or None})
    32→    host.save_plan(plan)
    33→    print(colorize(f"  Set description on {len(issue_ids)} issue(s).", "green"))
    34→
    35→
    36→def cmd_plan_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    37→    """Set note on issues."""
    38→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    39→
    40→    state = host.command_runtime(args).state
    41→    if not host.require_completed_scan(state):
    42→        return
    43→
    44→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    45→    text: str | None = getattr(args, "text", None)
    46→
    47→    plan = host.load_plan()
    48→    issue_ids = host.resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    49→    if not issue_ids:
    50→        print(colorize("  No matching issues found.", "yellow"))
    51→        return
    52→
    53→    for fid in issue_ids:
    54→        host.annotate_issue(plan, fid, text)
    55→    host.append_log_entry(plan, "note", issue_ids=issue_ids, actor="user", note=text)
    56→    host.save_plan(plan)
    57→    print(colorize(f"  Set note on {len(issue_ids)} issue(s).", "green"))
    58→
    59→
    60→def cmd_plan_reopen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    61→    """Reopen resolved issues from plan context."""
    62→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    63→
    64→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    65→
    66→    raw_state_path = host.state_path(args)
    67→    state_file = (
    68→        raw_state_path
    69→        if isinstance(raw_state_path, Path)
    70→        else Path(raw_state_path)
    71→        if raw_state_path
    72→        else None
    73→    )
    74→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    75→    plan_file = host._plan_file_for_state(state_file)
    76→
    77→    reopened: list[str] = []
    78→    for pattern in patterns:
    79→        reopened.extend(state_mod.resolve_issues(state_data, pattern, "open"))
    80→
    81→    if not reopened:
    82→        print(colorize("  No resolved issues matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
    83→        return
    84→
    85→    plan = host.load_plan(plan_file)
    86→    host.purge_uncommitted_ids(plan, reopened)
    87→
    88→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
    89→    count = 0
    90→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    91→    for fid in reopened:
    92→        if fid in skipped:
    93→            skipped.pop(fid)
    94→            count += 1
    95→        if fid not in order:
    96→            plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
    97→            order.add(fid)
    98→            count += 1
    99→
   100→    host.append_log_entry(plan, "reopen", issue_ids=reopened, actor="user")
   101→    host._save_plan_state_transactional(
   102→        plan=plan,
   103→        plan_path=plan_file,
   104→        state_data=state_data,
   105→        state_path_value=state_file,
   106→    )
   107→
   108→    print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} issue(s).", "green"))
   109→    if count:
   110→        print(colorize("  Plan updated: items moved back to queue.", "dim"))
   111→
   112→
   113→def cmd_plan_focus(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   114→    """Set or clear the active cluster focus."""
   115→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
   116→
   117→    clear_flag = getattr(args, "clear", False)
   118→    cluster_name: str | None = getattr(args, "cluster_name", None)
   119→
   120→    plan = host.load_plan()
   121→    if clear_flag:
   122→        prev = plan.get("active_cluster")
   123→        host.clear_focus(plan)
   124→        host.append_log_entry(plan, "focus", actor="user", detail={"action": "clear", "previous": prev})
   125→        host.save_plan(plan)
   126→        print(colorize("  Focus cleared.", "green"))
   127→        return
   128→
   129→    if not cluster_name:
   130→        active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   131→        if active:
   132→            print(f"  Focused on: {active}")
   133→        else:
   134→            print("  No active focus.")
   135→        return
   136→
   137→    try:
   138→        host.set_focus(plan, cluster_name)
   139→    except ValueError as ex:
   140→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   141→        return
   142→    host.append_log_entry(plan, "focus", cluster_name=cluster_name, actor="user", detail={"action": "set"})
   143→    host.save_plan(plan)
   144→    print(colorize(f"  Focused on: {cluster_name}", "green"))
   145→
   146→
   147→def cmd_plan_scan_gate(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   148→    """Check or skip the scan requirement for workflow items."""
   149→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
   150→
   151→    skip = getattr(args, "skip", False)
   152→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   153→
   154→    plan = host.load_plan()
   155→    scan_count_at_start = plan.get("scan_count_at_plan_start")
   156→
   157→    if scan_count_at_start is None:
   158→        print(colorize("  No active plan cycle (plan_start_scores not seeded).", "dim"))
   159→        print(colorize("  Scan gate is not applicable — workflow items gate themselves.", "dim"))
   160→        return
   161→
   162→    resolved_state_path = host.state_path(args)
   163→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(resolved_state_path)
   164→    current_scan_count = int(state_data.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   165→    scan_ran = current_scan_count > scan_count_at_start
   166→    scan_skipped = plan.get("scan_gate_skipped", False)
   167→
   168→    if not skip:
   169→        if scan_ran:
   170→            print(colorize("  Scan gate: PASSED", "green"))
   171→            print(colorize(f"  Scans at cycle start: {scan_count_at_start}  Current: {current_scan_count}", "dim"))
   172→        elif scan_skipped:
   173→            print(colorize("  Scan gate: SKIPPED (manually)", "yellow"))
   174→        else:
   175→            print(colorize("  Scan gate: BLOCKED", "red"))
   176→            print(colorize(f"  Scans at cycle start: {scan_count_at_start}  Current: {current_scan_count}", "dim"))
   177→            print(colorize("  Run: desloppify scan", "dim"))
   178→            print(colorize('  Or:  desloppify plan scan-gate --skip --note "reason"', "dim"))
   179→        return
   180→
   181→    if scan_ran:
   182→        print(colorize("  Scan already ran this cycle — no skip needed.", "green"))
   183→        return
   184→
   185→    if not note or len(note.strip()) < 50:
   186→        print(colorize("  --skip requires --note with at least 50 chars explaining why.", "red"))
   187→        return
   188→
   189→    plan["scan_gate_skipped"] = True
   190→    host.append_log_entry(
   191→        plan,
   192→        "scan_gate_skip",
   193→        actor="user",
   194→        note=note,
   195→        detail={
   196→            "scan_count_at_start": scan_count_at_start,
   197→            "current_scan_count": current_scan_count,
   198→        },
   199→    )
   200→    host.save_plan(plan)
   201→    print(colorize("  Scan requirement marked as satisfied (logged).", "yellow"))
   202→
   203→
   204→__all__ = [
   205→    "cmd_plan_describe",
   206→    "cmd_plan_focus",
   207→    "cmd_plan_note",
   208→    "cmd_plan_reopen",
   209→    "cmd_plan_scan_gate",
   210→]
   211→
```

```
1→"""Skip and unskip command handlers for plan overrides."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    10→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    11→from desloppify.base.output.user_message import print_user_message
    12→
    13→
    14→def _validate_skip_requirements(
    15→    *,
    16→    kind: str,
    17→    attestation: str | None,
    18→    note: str | None,
    19→) -> bool:
    20→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    21→
    22→    if not host.skip_kind_requires_attestation(kind):
    23→        return True
    24→    if not host.validate_attestation(attestation):
    25→        host.show_attestation_requirement(
    26→            "Permanent skip" if kind == "permanent" else "False positive",
    27→            attestation,
    28→            host.ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
    29→        )
    30→        return False
    31→    if host.skip_kind_requires_note(kind) and not note:
    32→        print(
    33→            colorize("  --permanent requires --note to explain the decision.", "yellow"),
    34→            file=sys.stderr,
    35→        )
    36→        return False
    37→    return True
    38→
    39→
    40→def _apply_state_skip_resolution(
    41→    *,
    42→    kind: str,
    43→    state_file: Path | None,
    44→    issue_ids: list[str],
    45→    note: str | None,
    46→    attestation: str | None,
    47→) -> dict | None:
    48→    status = _skip_kind_state_status(kind)
    49→    if status is None:
    50→        return None
    51→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    52→    for fid in issue_ids:
    53→        state_mod.resolve_issues(
    54→            state_data,
    55→            fid,
    56→            status,
    57→            note or "",
    58→            attestation=attestation,
    59→        )
    60→    return state_data
    61→
    62→
    63→def _skip_kind_state_status(kind: str) -> str | None:
    64→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    65→
    66→    return host.skip_kind_state_status(kind)
    67→
    68→
    69→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    70→    """Skip issues — unified command for temporary/permanent/false-positive."""
    71→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    72→
    73→    runtime = host.command_runtime(args)
    74→    state = runtime.state
    75→    if not host.require_completed_scan(state):
    76→        return
    77→
    78→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    79→    reason: str | None = getattr(args, "reason", None)
    80→    review_after: int | None = getattr(args, "review_after", None)
    81→    permanent: bool = getattr(args, "permanent", False)
    82→    false_positive: bool = getattr(args, "false_positive", False)
    83→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
    84→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    85→
    86→    kind = host.skip_kind_from_flags(permanent=permanent, false_positive=false_positive)
    87→    if not _validate_skip_requirements(kind=kind, attestation=attestation, note=note):
    88→        return
    89→
    90→    state_file = runtime.state_path
    91→    plan_file = host._plan_file_for_state(state_file)
    92→    plan = host.load_plan(plan_file)
    93→    issue_ids = host.resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    94→    if not issue_ids:
    95→        print(colorize("  No matching issues found.", "yellow"))
    96→        return
    97→
    98→    if len(issue_ids) > host._BULK_SKIP_THRESHOLD:
    99→        print(
   100→            colorize(
   101→                f"  Bulk skip: {len(issue_ids)} items will be removed from the active queue.",
   102→                "yellow",
   103→            ),
   104→            file=sys.stderr,
   105→        )
   106→        if not getattr(args, "confirm", False):
   107→            raise host.CommandError(
   108→                f"Skipping {len(issue_ids)} items requires --confirm. "
   109→                "Review the items first, or skip individually."
   110→            )
   111→
   112→    state_data = _apply_state_skip_resolution(
   113→        kind=kind,
   114→        state_file=state_file,
   115→        issue_ids=issue_ids,
   116→        note=note,
   117→        attestation=attestation,
   118→    )
   119→
   120→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   121→    count = host.skip_items(
   122→        plan,
   123→        issue_ids,
   124→        kind=kind,
   125→        reason=reason,
   126→        note=note,
   127→        attestation=attestation,
   128→        review_after=review_after,
   129→        scan_count=scan_count,
   130→    )
   131→
   132→    host.append_log_entry(
   133→        plan,
   134→        "skip",
   135→        issue_ids=issue_ids,
   136→        actor="user",
   137→        note=note,
   138→        detail={"kind": kind, "reason": reason},
   139→    )
   140→    if state_data is not None:
   141→        host._save_plan_state_transactional(
   142→            plan=plan,
   143→            plan_path=plan_file,
   144→            state_data=state_data,
   145→            state_path_value=state_file,
   146→        )
   147→    else:
   148→        host.save_plan(plan, plan_file)
   149→
   150→    print(colorize(f"  {host.SKIP_KIND_LABELS[kind]} {count} item(s).", "green"))
   151→    if review_after:
   152→        print(colorize(f"  Will re-surface after {review_after} scan(s).", "dim"))
   153→    print_user_message(
   154→        "Hey — if skipping was the right call, just continue with"
   155→        " what you were doing. If you think a broader re-triage is"
   156→        " needed, use `desloppify plan triage`. Run `desloppify"
   157→        " plan --help` to see all available plan tools. Otherwise"
   158→        " no need to reply, just keep going."
   159→    )
   160→
   161→
   162→def cmd_plan_unskip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   163→    """Unskip issues — bring back to queue."""
   164→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
   165→
   166→    runtime = host.command_runtime(args)
   167→    state = runtime.state
   168→    if not host.require_completed_scan(state):
   169→        return
   170→
   171→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   172→
   173→    state_file = runtime.state_path
   174→    plan_file = host._plan_file_for_state(state_file)
   175→    plan = host.load_plan(plan_file)
   176→    issue_ids = host.resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan, status_filter="all")
   177→    if not issue_ids:
   178→        print(colorize("  No matching issues found.", "yellow"))
   179→        return
   180→
   181→    include_protected = bool(getattr(args, "force", False))
   182→    count, need_reopen, protected_kept = host.unskip_items(
   183→        plan,
   184→        issue_ids,
   185→        include_protected=include_protected,
   186→    )
   187→    unskipped_ids = [fid for fid in issue_ids if fid not in protected_kept]
   188→    host.append_log_entry(
   189→        plan,
   190→        "unskip",
   191→        issue_ids=unskipped_ids,
   192→        actor="user",
   193→        detail={"need_reopen": need_reopen},
   194→    )
   195→
   196→    reopened: list[str] = []
   197→    if need_reopen:
   198→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   199→        for fid in need_reopen:
   200→            reopened.extend(state_mod.resolve_issues(state_data, fid, "open"))
   201→        host._save_plan_state_transactional(
   202→            plan=plan,
   203→            plan_path=plan_file,
   204→            state_data=state_data,
   205→            state_path_value=state_file,
   206→        )
   207→        print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} issue(s) in state.", "dim"))
   208→    else:
   209→        host.save_plan(plan, plan_file)
   210→
   211→    print(colorize(f"  Unskipped {count} item(s) — back in queue.", "green"))
   212→    if protected_kept:
   213→        print(
   214→            colorize(
   215→                f"  Kept {len(protected_kept)} protected skip(s) "
   216→                f"(permanent/false_positive with notes). Use --force to override.",
   217→                "yellow",
   218→            )
   219→        )
   220→
   221→
   222→__all__ = [

   224→    "_validate_skip_requirements",
   225→    "cmd_plan_skip",

   227→]
```

```
    16→def cmd_plan_resolve(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:

    18→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415

    36→        for sid in synthetic_ids:

    39→                print(colorize(f"  Cannot resolve {sid} — blocked by: {deps}", "red"))

    41→                if not getattr(args, "force_resolve", False):

    43→
    44→        gated_ids = [

    53→            if triage_ever_completed:

    57→                required_stages = {"observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich", "commit"}
    58→                missing = required_stages - confirmed_stages
    59→
    60→            if missing and not force:

    62→                    host.inject_triage_stages(plan)
    63→                    meta.setdefault("triage_stages", {})
    64→                    plan["epic_triage_meta"] = meta
    65→                    host.save_plan(plan)
    66→
    67→                stage_order = ["observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich", "commit"]
    68→                next_stage = next((stage for stage in stage_order if stage in missing), "observe")

    70→                for wid in gated_ids:

    73→

    85→                elif next_stage == "organize":

    90→                    print(colorize('    desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "..."', "dim"))
    91→                    print()

    93→                elif next_stage == "enrich":

    96→                    print(colorize('    desloppify plan triage --stage enrich --report "..."', "dim"))
```

```
1→"""Helper logic for plan resolve command workflow/triage gating."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     6→
     7→
     8→def check_cluster_guard(patterns: list[str], plan: dict, state: dict) -> bool:
     9→    """Return True if blocked by cluster guard, False if OK to proceed."""
    10→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    11→
    12→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    13→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    14→    for pattern in patterns:
    15→        if pattern in clusters:
    16→            cluster = clusters[pattern]
    17→            ids = [
    18→                fid
    19→                for fid in cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
    20→                if fid in issues and issues[fid].get("status") == "open"
    21→            ]
    22→            if len(ids) == 0:
    23→                print(
    24→                    colorize(
    25→                        f"\n  Cluster '{pattern}' is empty — add items before marking it done.\n",
    26→                        "yellow",
    27→                    )
    28→                )
    29→                print(colorize(f"  Use: desloppify plan cluster add {pattern} <issue-id>", "dim"))
    30→                return True
    31→            if len(ids) <= host._CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD:
    32→                print_cluster_guard(pattern, ids, state)
    33→                return True
    34→    return False
    35→
    36→
    37→def print_cluster_guard(cluster_name: str, issue_ids: list[str], state: dict) -> None:
    38→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    39→    print(
    40→        colorize(
    41→            f"\n  Cluster '{cluster_name}' has {len(issue_ids)} item(s) — mark them done individually first:\n",
    42→            "yellow",
    43→        )
    44→    )
    45→    for fid in issue_ids:
    46→        issue = issues.get(fid, {})
    47→        summary = issue.get("summary", "(no summary)")[:80]
    48→        detector = issue.get("detector", "?")
    49→        print(f"    {fid}  [{detector}]  {summary}")
    50→    print(
    51→        colorize(
    52→            "\n  Use: desloppify resolve <id> --status fixed --note '...' --attest '...'",
    53→            "dim",
    54→        )
    55→    )
    56→    print(
    57→        colorize(
    58→            "  Or mark each resolved: desloppify plan resolve <id> --note '...' --confirm\n",
    59→            "dim",
    60→        )
    61→    )
    62→
    63→
    64→def is_synthetic_id(fid: str) -> bool:
    65→    """Return True if the ID is a synthetic workflow/triage item."""
    66→    return fid.startswith("triage::") or fid.startswith("workflow::") or fid.startswith("subjective::")
    67→
    68→
    69→def resolve_synthetic_ids(patterns: list[str]) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
    70→    """Separate synthetic IDs from real issue patterns."""
    71→    synthetic = [pattern for pattern in patterns if is_synthetic_id(pattern)]
    72→    remaining = [pattern for pattern in patterns if not is_synthetic_id(pattern)]
    73→    return synthetic, remaining
    74→
    75→
    76→def blocked_triage_stages(plan: dict) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
    77→    """Return triage stages that are blocked by unmet dependencies."""
    78→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    79→
    80→    order_set = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    81→    present = order_set & host.TRIAGE_IDS
    82→    if not present:
    83→        return {}
    84→
    85→    confirmed = set(plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {}).get("triage_stages", {}).keys())
    86→    stage_names = ("observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich", "sense-check", "commit")
    87→
    88→    blocked: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    89→    for sid, name in zip(host.TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS, stage_names, strict=False):
    90→        if sid not in present or name in confirmed:
    91→            continue
    92→        deps = host.TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES.get(name, set())
    93→        unmet = sorted(
    94→            f"triage::{dep}" for dep in deps if f"triage::{dep}" in present and dep not in confirmed
    95→        )
    96→        if unmet:
    97→            blocked[sid] = unmet
    98→    return blocked
    99→
   100→
   101→__all__ = [
   102→    "blocked_triage_stages",
   103→    "check_cluster_guard",
   104→    "resolve_synthetic_ids",
   105→]
   106→
```

```
1→"""Small utility helpers for concern generation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from .types import ConcernSignals, SignalKey
     9→
    10→
    11→def _update_max_signal(signals: ConcernSignals, key: SignalKey, value: object) -> None:
    12→    """Update numeric signal key with max(existing, value) when value is valid."""
    13→    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int | float) or value <= 0:
    14→        return
    15→    current = float(signals.get(key, 0.0))
    16→    signals[key] = max(current, float(value))
    17→
    18→
    19→def _fingerprint(concern_type: str, file: str, key_signals: tuple[str, ...]) -> str:
    20→    """Stable hash of (type, file, sorted key signals)."""
    21→    raw = f"{concern_type}::{file}::{','.join(sorted(key_signals))}"
    22→    return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    23→
    24→
    25→def _is_dismissed(
    26→    dismissals: dict[str, Any], fingerprint: str, source_issue_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    27→) -> bool:
    28→    """Check if a concern was previously dismissed and source issues unchanged."""
    29→    entry = dismissals.get(fingerprint)
    30→    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    31→        return False
    32→    prev_sources = set(entry.get("source_issue_ids", []))
    33→    return prev_sources == set(source_issue_ids)
    34→
    35→
    36→__all__ = ["_fingerprint", "_is_dismissed", "_update_max_signal"]
    37→
```

```
     4→
     5→from collections import defaultdict
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
     9→
    10→
    11→def _open_issues(state: StateModel) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    12→    """Return all open issues from state."""
    13→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    14→    return [
    15→        finding for finding in issues.values()
    16→        if isinstance(finding, dict) and finding.get("status") == "open"
    17→    ]
    18→
    19→
    20→def _group_by_file(state: StateModel) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]:
    21→    """Group open issues by file, excluding holistic (file='.') issues."""
    22→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
    23→    for finding in _open_issues(state):
    24→        file = finding.get("file", "")
    25→        if file and file != ".":
    26→            by_file[file].append(finding)
    27→    return dict(by_file)
    28→
    29→
    30→__all__ = ["_group_by_file", "_open_issues"]
    31→
```

```
1→"""Signal extraction helpers for concern generation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from .constants import ELEVATED_LOC, ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING, ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS
     9→from .types import ConcernSignals
    10→from .utils import _update_max_signal
    11→
    12→
    13→def _parse_complexity_signals(detail: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, float]:
    14→    """Parse params/nesting labels from structural complexity signals."""
    15→    result: dict[str, float] = {}
    16→    raw_signals = detail.get("complexity_signals", [])
    17→    if not isinstance(raw_signals, list):
    18→        return result
    19→
    20→    for signal in raw_signals:
    21→        if not isinstance(signal, str):
    22→            continue
    23→        params = re.search(r"(\d+)\s*params", signal)
    24→        if params:
    25→            result["max_params"] = max(
    26→                result.get("max_params", 0),
    27→                float(params.group(1)),
    28→            )
    29→        nesting = re.search(r"nesting depth\s*(\d+)", signal)
    30→        if nesting:
    31→            result["max_nesting"] = max(
    32→                result.get("max_nesting", 0),
    33→                float(nesting.group(1)),
    34→            )
    35→    return result
    36→
    37→
    38→def _extract_signals(issues: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> ConcernSignals:
    39→    """Extract key quantitative signals from a file's issues."""
    40→    signals: ConcernSignals = {}
    41→    monster_funcs: list[str] = []
    42→
    43→    for finding in issues:
    44→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    45→        detail_raw = finding.get("detail", {})
    46→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
    47→
    48→        if detector == "structural":
    49→            _update_max_signal(signals, "loc", detail.get("loc", 0))
    50→            parsed = _parse_complexity_signals(detail)
    51→            for key in ("max_params", "max_nesting"):
    52→                if key in parsed:
    53→                    _update_max_signal(signals, key, parsed[key])
    54→
    55→        if detector == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
    56→            _update_max_signal(signals, "monster_loc", detail.get("loc", 0))
    57→            function_name = detail.get("function", "")
    58→            if isinstance(function_name, str) and function_name:
    59→                monster_funcs.append(function_name)
    60→
    61→    if monster_funcs:
    62→        signals["monster_funcs"] = monster_funcs
    63→    return signals
    64→
    65→
    66→def _has_elevated_signals(issues: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
    67→    """Return whether any issue has strong enough signals to flag on its own."""
    68→    for finding in issues:
    69→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    70→        detail_raw = finding.get("detail", {})
    71→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
    72→
    73→        if detector == "structural":
    74→            if detail.get("loc", 0) >= ELEVATED_LOC:
    75→                return True
    76→            parsed = _parse_complexity_signals(detail)
    77→            if parsed.get("max_params", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
    78→                return True
    79→            if parsed.get("max_nesting", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
    80→                return True
    81→
    82→        if detector == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
    83→            return True
    84→
    85→        if detector in (
    86→            "dupes",
    87→            "boilerplate_duplication",
    88→            "coupling",
    89→            "responsibility_cohesion",
    90→        ):
    91→            return True
    92→
    93→    return False
    94→
    95→
    96→__all__ = ["_extract_signals", "_has_elevated_signals", "_parse_complexity_signals"]
    97→
```

```
1→"""Concern classification and text construction helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Callable
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from .constants import ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING, ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS, MIN_DETECTORS_FOR_MIXED
     9→from .types import ConcernSignals
    10→
    11→
    12→def _classify(detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals) -> str:
    13→    """Pick the most specific concern type from what's present."""
    14→    if len(detectors) >= MIN_DETECTORS_FOR_MIXED:
    15→        return "mixed_responsibilities"
    16→    if "dupes" in detectors or "boilerplate_duplication" in detectors:
    17→        return "duplication_design"
    18→    if signals.get("monster_loc", 0) > 0:
    19→        return "structural_complexity"
    20→    if "coupling" in detectors:
    21→        return "coupling_design"
    22→    if signals.get("max_params", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
    23→        return "interface_design"
    24→    if signals.get("max_nesting", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
    25→        return "structural_complexity"
    26→    if "responsibility_cohesion" in detectors:
    27→        return "mixed_responsibilities"
    28→    return "design_concern"
    29→
    30→
    31→_SUMMARY_TEMPLATES: dict[str, str] = {
    32→    "mixed_responsibilities": (
    33→        "Issues from {detector_count} detectors — may have too many responsibilities"
    34→    ),
    35→    "duplication_design": "Duplication pattern — assess if extraction is warranted",
    36→    "coupling_design": "Coupling pattern — assess if boundaries need adjustment",
    37→    "interface_design": "Interface complexity: {max_params} parameters",
    38→}
    39→_DEFAULT_SUMMARY_TEMPLATE = "Design signals from {detector_list}"
    40→
    41→
    42→def _summary_context(detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals) -> dict[str, object]:
    43→    return {
    44→        "detector_count": len(detectors),
    45→        "detector_list": ", ".join(sorted(detectors)),
    46→        "max_params": int(signals.get("max_params", 0)),
    47→    }
    48→
    49→
    50→def _build_structural_summary(signals: ConcernSignals) -> str:
    51→    parts: list[str] = []
    52→    monster_loc = signals.get("monster_loc", 0)
    53→    if monster_loc:
    54→        funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
    55→        label = f" ({', '.join(funcs[:3])})" if funcs else ""
    56→        parts.append(f"monster function{label}: {int(monster_loc)} lines")
    57→    nesting = signals.get("max_nesting", 0)
    58→    if nesting >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
    59→        parts.append(f"nesting depth {int(nesting)}")
    60→    params = signals.get("max_params", 0)
    61→    if params >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
    62→        parts.append(f"{int(params)} parameters")
    63→    return f"Structural complexity: {', '.join(parts) or 'elevated signals'}"
    64→
    65→
    66→def _build_summary(
    67→    concern_type: str,
    68→    detectors: set[str],
    69→    signals: ConcernSignals,
    70→) -> str:
    71→    """Build a one-line concern summary."""
    72→    if concern_type == "structural_complexity":
    73→        return _build_structural_summary(signals)
    74→    template = _SUMMARY_TEMPLATES.get(concern_type, _DEFAULT_SUMMARY_TEMPLATE)
    75→    return template.format(**_summary_context(detectors, signals))
    76→
    77→
    78→def _build_evidence(
    79→    issues: list[dict[str, Any]],
    80→    signals: ConcernSignals,
    81→) -> tuple[str, ...]:
    82→    """Build evidence tuple from all issues and extracted signals."""
    83→    evidence: list[str] = []
    84→
    85→    detectors = sorted({finding.get("detector", "") for finding in issues})
    86→    evidence.append(f"Flagged by: {', '.join(detectors)}")
    87→
    88→    loc = signals.get("loc")
    89→    if loc:
    90→        evidence.append(f"File size: {int(loc)} lines")
    91→    params = signals.get("max_params")
    92→    if params and params >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
    93→        evidence.append(f"Max parameters: {int(params)}")
    94→    nesting = signals.get("max_nesting")
    95→    if nesting and nesting >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
    96→        evidence.append(f"Max nesting depth: {int(nesting)}")
    97→    monster_loc = signals.get("monster_loc")
    98→    if monster_loc:
    99→        funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   100→        label = f" ({', '.join(funcs[:3])})" if funcs else ""
   101→        evidence.append(f"Monster function{label}: {int(monster_loc)} lines")
   102→
   103→    for finding in issues[:10]:
   104→        summary = finding.get("summary", "")
   105→        if summary:
   106→            evidence.append(f"[{finding.get('detector', '')}] {summary}")
   107→
   108→    return tuple(evidence)
   109→
   110→
   111→def _build_question(detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals) -> str:
   112→    """Build a targeted question from dominant detector and signal patterns."""
   113→    funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   114→    context = {
   115→        "detector_count": len(detectors),
   116→        "detector_list": ", ".join(sorted(detectors)),
   117→        "first_monster_func": funcs[0] if funcs else "",
   118→    }
   119→
   120→    question_rules: tuple[
   121→        tuple[Callable[[set[str], ConcernSignals], bool], str],
   122→        ...,
   123→    ] = (
   124→        (
   125→            lambda dets, _signals: len(dets) >= MIN_DETECTORS_FOR_MIXED,
   126→            (
   127→                "This file has issues across {detector_count} dimensions "
   128→                "({detector_list}). Is it trying to do too many things, "
   129→                "or is this complexity inherent to its domain?"
   130→            ),
   131→        ),
   132→        (
   133→            lambda _dets, sig: bool(sig.get("monster_funcs")),
   134→            (
   135→                "What are the distinct responsibilities in {first_monster_func}()? "
   136→                "Should it be decomposed into focused functions?"
   137→            ),
   138→        ),
   139→        (
   140→            lambda _dets, sig: sig.get("max_params", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS,
   141→            (
   142→                "Should the parameters be grouped into a config/context object? "
   143→                "Which ones belong together?"
   144→            ),
   145→        ),
   146→        (
   147→            lambda _dets, sig: sig.get("max_nesting", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING,
   148→            (
   149→                "Can the nesting be reduced with early returns, guard clauses, "
   150→                "or extraction into helper functions?"
   151→            ),
   152→        ),
   153→        (
   154→            lambda dets, _signals: "dupes" in dets or "boilerplate_duplication" in dets,
   155→            (
   156→                "Is the duplication worth extracting into a shared utility, "
   157→                "or is it intentional variation?"
   158→            ),
   159→        ),
   160→        (
   161→            lambda dets, _signals: "coupling" in dets,
   162→            (
   163→                "Is the coupling intentional or does it indicate a missing "
   164→                "abstraction boundary?"
   165→            ),
   166→        ),
   167→        (
   168→            lambda dets, _signals: "orphaned" in dets,
   169→            (
   170→                "Is this file truly dead, or is it used via a non-import mechanism "
   171→                "(dynamic import, CLI entry point, plugin)?"
   172→            ),
   173→        ),
   174→        (
   175→            lambda dets, _signals: "responsibility_cohesion" in dets,
   176→            (
   177→                "What are the distinct responsibilities? Would splitting "
   178→                "produce modules with multiple independent consumers, or "
   179→                "would extracted files only be imported by the parent? "
   180→                "Only split if the extracted code would be reused."
   181→            ),
   182→        ),
   183→    )
   184→    parts = [
   185→        template.format(**context)
   186→        for predicate, template in question_rules
   187→        if predicate(detectors, signals)
   188→    ]
   189→    if parts:
   190→        return " ".join(parts)
   191→
   192→    return (
   193→        "Review the flagged patterns — are they design problems that "
   194→        "need addressing, or acceptable given the file's role?"
   195→    )
   196→
   197→
   198→__all__ = [
   199→    "_build_evidence",
   200→    "_build_question",
   201→    "_build_summary",
   202→    "_classify",
   203→]
   204→
```

```
1→"""Concern generators bridging mechanical findings to holistic review cues."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections import defaultdict
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.registry import JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
     9→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    10→
    11→from .constants import MIN_FILES_FOR_SMELL_PATTERN, MIN_FILES_FOR_SYSTEMIC
    12→from .signals import _extract_signals, _has_elevated_signals
    13→from .state import _group_by_file, _open_issues
    14→from .text import _build_evidence, _build_question, _build_summary, _classify
    15→from .types import Concern
    16→from .utils import _fingerprint, _is_dismissed
    17→
    18→
    19→def _try_make_concern(
    20→    *,
    21→    concern_type: str,
    22→    file: str,
    23→    fp_keys: tuple[str, ...],
    24→    all_ids: tuple[str, ...],
    25→    dismissals: dict[str, Any],
    26→    summary: str,
    27→    evidence: tuple[str, ...],
    28→    question: str,
    29→    fp_file: str | None = None,
    30→) -> Concern | None:
    31→    """Create a concern unless a matching dismissal exists."""
    32→    fp = _fingerprint(concern_type, fp_file if fp_file is not None else file, fp_keys)
    33→    if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
    34→        return None
    35→    return Concern(
    36→        type=concern_type,
    37→        file=file,
    38→        summary=summary,
    39→        evidence=evidence,
    40→        question=question,
    41→        fingerprint=fp,
    42→        source_issues=all_ids,
    43→    )
    44→
    45→
    46→def _file_concerns(state: StateModel, dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
    47→    """Build per-file concerns from aggregated judgment detector signals."""
    48→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
    49→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
    50→
    51→    for file, all_issues in by_file.items():
    52→        judgment = [
    53→            finding
    54→            for finding in all_issues
    55→            if finding.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
    56→        ]
    57→        if not judgment:
    58→            continue
    59→
    60→        judgment_dets = {finding.get("detector", "") for finding in judgment}
    61→        elevated = _has_elevated_signals(judgment)
    62→
    63→        mechanical_count = len(all_issues)
    64→        if len(judgment_dets) < 2 and not elevated:
    65→            if not (len(judgment_dets) >= 1 and mechanical_count >= 3):
    66→                continue
    67→
    68→        signals = _extract_signals(judgment)
    69→        concern_type = _classify(judgment_dets, signals)
    70→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(finding.get("id", "") for finding in judgment))
    71→        fp_keys = tuple(sorted(judgment_dets))
    72→
    73→        concern = _try_make_concern(
    74→            concern_type=concern_type,
    75→            file=file,
    76→            fp_keys=fp_keys,
    77→            all_ids=all_ids,
    78→            dismissals=dismissals,
    79→            summary=_build_summary(concern_type, judgment_dets, signals),
    80→            evidence=_build_evidence(judgment, signals),
    81→            question=_build_question(judgment_dets, signals),
    82→        )
    83→        if concern is not None:
    84→            concerns.append(concern)
    85→
    86→    return concerns
    87→
    88→
    89→def _cross_file_patterns(state: StateModel, dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
    90→    """Build systemic concerns for detector-combo patterns across files."""
    91→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
    92→
    93→    profile_to_files: dict[frozenset[str], list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
    94→    for file, issues in by_file.items():
    95→        detectors = frozenset(
    96→            finding.get("detector", "")
    97→            for finding in issues
    98→            if finding.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
    99→        )
   100→        if len(detectors) >= 2:
   101→            profile_to_files[detectors].append(file)
   102→
   103→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   104→    for det_combo, files in profile_to_files.items():
   105→        if len(files) < MIN_FILES_FOR_SYSTEMIC:
   106→            continue
   107→
   108→        sorted_files = sorted(files)
   109→        combo_names = tuple(sorted(det_combo))
   110→        all_ids = tuple(
   111→            sorted(
   112→                finding.get("id", "")
   113→                for file in sorted_files
   114→                for finding in by_file[file]
   115→                if finding.get("detector", "") in det_combo
   116→            )
   117→        )
   118→        concern = _try_make_concern(
   119→            concern_type="systemic_pattern",
   120→            file=sorted_files[0],
   121→            fp_file=",".join(sorted_files[:5]),
   122→            fp_keys=combo_names,
   123→            all_ids=all_ids,
   124→            dismissals=dismissals,
   125→            summary=(
   126→                f"{len(files)} files share the same problem pattern "
   127→                f"({', '.join(combo_names)})"
   128→            ),
   129→            evidence=(
   130→                f"Affected files: {', '.join(sorted_files[:10])}",
   131→                f"Shared detectors: {', '.join(combo_names)}",
   132→                f"Total files: {len(files)}",
   133→            ),
   134→            question=(
   135→                f"These {len(files)} files all have the same combination "
   136→                f"of issues ({', '.join(combo_names)}). Is this a systemic "
   137→                "pattern that should be addressed at the architecture level "
   138→                "(shared base class, framework change, lint rule), or are "
   139→                "these independent issues that happen to look similar?"
   140→            ),
   141→        )
   142→        if concern is not None:
   143→            concerns.append(concern)
   144→
   145→    return concerns
   146→
   147→
   148→def _systemic_smell_patterns(
   149→    state: StateModel,
   150→    dismissals: dict[str, Any],
   151→) -> list[Concern]:
   152→    """Build systemic concerns when a single smell appears across many files."""
   153→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   154→    smell_ids_map: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   155→
   156→    for finding in _open_issues(state):
   157→        if finding.get("detector") != "smells":
   158→            continue
   159→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   160→        smell_id = detail.get("smell_id", "") if isinstance(detail, dict) else ""
   161→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   162→        if smell_id and filepath and filepath != ".":
   163→            smell_files[smell_id].append(filepath)
   164→            smell_ids_map[smell_id].append(finding.get("id", ""))
   165→
   166→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   167→    for smell_id, files in smell_files.items():
   168→        unique_files = sorted(set(files))
   169→        if len(unique_files) < MIN_FILES_FOR_SMELL_PATTERN:
   170→            continue
   171→
   172→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(smell_ids_map[smell_id]))
   173→        concern = _try_make_concern(
   174→            concern_type="systemic_smell",
   175→            file=unique_files[0],
   176→            fp_file=smell_id,
   177→            fp_keys=(smell_id,),
   178→            all_ids=all_ids,
   179→            dismissals=dismissals,
   180→            summary=(
   181→                f"'{smell_id}' appears in {len(unique_files)} files — "
   182→                "likely a systemic pattern"
   183→            ),
   184→            evidence=(
   185→                f"Smell: {smell_id}",
   186→                f"Affected files ({len(unique_files)}): {', '.join(unique_files[:10])}",
   187→            ),
   188→            question=(
   189→                f"The smell '{smell_id}' appears across {len(unique_files)} files. "
   190→                "Is this a codebase-wide convention that should be addressed "
   191→                "systemically (lint rule, shared utility, architecture change), "
   192→                "or are these independent occurrences?"
   193→            ),
   194→        )
   195→        if concern is not None:
   196→            concerns.append(concern)
   197→
   198→    return concerns
   199→
   200→
   201→_GENERATORS = [_file_concerns, _cross_file_patterns, _systemic_smell_patterns]
   202→
   203→
   204→def generate_concerns(state: StateModel) -> list[Concern]:
   205→    """Run all concern generators against current state."""
   206→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   207→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   208→    seen_fingerprints: set[str] = set()
   209→
   210→    for generator in _GENERATORS:
   211→        for concern in generator(state, dismissals):
   212→            if concern.fingerprint not in seen_fingerprints:
   213→                seen_fingerprints.add(concern.fingerprint)
   214→                concerns.append(concern)
   215→
   216→    concerns.sort(key=lambda concern: (concern.type, concern.file))
   217→    return concerns
   218→
   219→
   220→def cleanup_stale_dismissals(state: StateModel) -> int:
   221→    """Remove concern dismissals whose source issues all disappeared."""
   222→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   223→    if not dismissals:
   224→        return 0
   225→    open_ids = {finding.get("id", "") for finding in _open_issues(state)}
   226→    stale_fingerprints = [
   227→        fingerprint
   228→        for fingerprint, entry in dismissals.items()
   229→        if entry.get("source_issue_ids")
   230→        and not any(source_id in open_ids for source_id in entry["source_issue_ids"])
   231→    ]
   232→    for fingerprint in stale_fingerprints:
   233→        del dismissals[fingerprint]
   234→    return len(stale_fingerprints)
   235→
   236→
   237→__all__ = [
   238→    "_cross_file_patterns",
   239→    "_file_concerns",
   240→    "cleanup_stale_dismissals",
   241→    "generate_concerns",
   242→]
   243→
```

```
1→"""Direct coverage tests for holistic budget pattern wrapper scanners."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import ast
     6→
     7→from desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic import (
     8→    budget_patterns_wrappers as wrappers_mod,
     9→)
    10→
    11→
    12→def _parse(content: str) -> ast.Module:
    13→    return ast.parse(content)
    14→
    15→
    16→def test_budget_patterns_wrappers_exports_expected_symbols() -> None:
    17→    assert set(wrappers_mod.__all__) == {
    18→        "_find_delegation_heavy_classes",
    19→        "_find_facade_modules",
    20→        "_find_python_passthrough_wrappers",
    21→        "_is_delegation_stmt",
    22→        "_python_passthrough_target",
    23→    }
    24→
    25→
    26→def test_python_passthrough_target_detects_return_call_name() -> None:
    27→    stmt = _parse("def f():\n    return build(x)\n").body[0].body[0]
    28→    assert wrappers_mod._python_passthrough_target(stmt) == "build"
    29→
    30→    non_match = _parse("def f():\n    value = build(x)\n").body[0].body[0]
    31→    assert wrappers_mod._python_passthrough_target(non_match) is None
    32→
    33→
    34→def test_is_delegation_stmt_detects_self_attribute_chain() -> None:
    35→    stmt = _parse("def f(self):\n    return self.inner.service.run()\n").body[0].body[0]
    36→    assert wrappers_mod._is_delegation_stmt(stmt) == "inner"
    37→
    38→    non_match = _parse("def f(obj):\n    return obj.inner.run()\n").body[0].body[0]
    39→    assert wrappers_mod._is_delegation_stmt(non_match) is None
    40→
    41→
    42→def test_find_python_passthrough_wrappers_reports_wrapper_pairs() -> None:
    43→    tree = _parse(
    44→        "def wrap_user(value):\n"
    45→        "    return build_user(value)\n\n"
    46→        "def build_user(value):\n"
    47→        "    return value\n"
    48→    )
    49→    assert wrappers_mod._find_python_passthrough_wrappers(tree) == [
    50→        ("wrap_user", "build_user"),
    51→    ]
    52→
    53→
    54→def test_find_facade_modules_detects_re_export_heavy_module() -> None:
    55→    tree = _parse(
    56→        "from x import A, B, C\n"
    57→        "from y import D\n"
    58→        "class Local: pass\n"
    59→    )
    60→    result = wrappers_mod._find_facade_modules(tree, loc=3)
    61→    assert result is not None
    62→    assert result["re_export_ratio"] >= 0.7
    63→    assert result["defined_symbols"] == 1
    64→
```

```
1→"""Completion strategy/policy validation helpers for triage workflow."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.app.commands.plan.triage_playbook import TRIAGE_CMD_ORGANIZE
     6→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     7→from desloppify.engine.plan import extract_issue_citations
     8→
     9→from .display import show_plan_summary
    10→from .helpers import manual_clusters_with_issues
    11→from .stage_helpers import unclustered_review_issues, unenriched_clusters
    12→
    13→
    14→def _completion_clusters_valid(plan: dict, state: dict | None = None) -> bool:
    15→    manual_clusters = manual_clusters_with_issues(plan)
    16→    if not manual_clusters:
    17→        any_clusters = [name for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items() if cluster.get("issue_ids")]
    18→        if not any_clusters:
    19→            print(colorize("  Cannot complete: no clusters with issues exist.", "red"))
    20→            print(colorize('  Create clusters: desloppify plan cluster create <name> --description "..."', "dim"))
    21→            return False
    22→
    23→    gaps = unenriched_clusters(plan)
    24→    if gaps:
    25→        print(colorize(f"  Cannot complete: {len(gaps)} cluster(s) still need enrichment.", "red"))
    26→        for name, missing in gaps:
    27→            print(colorize(f"    {name}: missing {', '.join(missing)}", "yellow"))
    28→        print(colorize("  Small clusters (<5 issues) need at least 1 action step per issue.", "dim"))
    29→        print(colorize('  Fix: desloppify plan cluster update <name> --description "..." --steps "step1" "step2"', "dim"))
    30→        return False
    31→
    32→    unclustered = unclustered_review_issues(plan, state)
    33→    if unclustered:
    34→        print(colorize(f"  Cannot complete: {len(unclustered)} review issue(s) have no action plan.", "red"))
    35→        for fid in unclustered[:5]:
    36→            short = fid.rsplit("::", 2)[-2] if "::" in fid else fid
    37→            print(colorize(f"    {short}", "yellow"))
    38→        if len(unclustered) > 5:
    39→            print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(unclustered) - 5} more", "yellow"))
    40→        print(colorize("  Add to a cluster or wontfix each unclustered issue.", "dim"))
```

```
1→"""Enrichment and cluster-level validation helpers for triage stages."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     9→
    10→_PATH_RE = re.compile(r"(?:src|supabase)/[\w./-]+\.\w+")
    11→_EXT_SWAPS = {".ts": ".tsx", ".tsx": ".ts", ".js": ".jsx", ".jsx": ".js"}
    12→_VALID_EFFORTS = {"trivial", "small", "medium", "large"}
    13→
    14→
    15→def _require_organize_stage_for_enrich(stages: dict) -> bool:
    16→    """Gate: organize must be done before enrich."""
    17→    if "organize" in stages:
    18→        return True
    19→    if "observe" not in stages:
    20→        print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: observe stage not complete.", "red"))
    21→        print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "..."', "dim"))
    22→        return False
    23→    if "reflect" not in stages:
    24→        print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: reflect stage not complete.", "red"))
    25→        print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "..."', "dim"))
    26→        return False
    27→    print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: organize stage not complete.", "red"))
    28→    print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "..."', "dim"))
    29→    return False
    30→
    31→
    32→def _underspecified_steps(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, int, int]]:
    33→    """Return (cluster_name, bare_count, total_count) for steps missing detail or issue_refs."""
    34→    results: list[tuple[str, int, int]] = []
    35→    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    36→        if cluster.get("auto") or not cluster.get("issue_ids"):
    37→            continue
    38→        steps = cluster.get("action_steps") or []
    39→        if not steps:
    40→            continue
```

```
1→"""Cluster action-step rendering helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def print_step(i: int, step: str | dict, *, colorize_fn) -> None:
     7→    """Print a single step with title, detail, refs, and done status."""
     8→    if isinstance(step, str):
     9→        print(colorize_fn(f"    {i}. {step}", "dim"))
    10→        return
    11→    done = step.get("done", False)
    12→    marker = "[x]" if done else "[ ]"
    13→    title = step.get("title", "")
    14→    print(f"    {i}. {marker} {title}")
    15→    if done:
    16→        print(colorize_fn("         (completed)", "dim"))
    17→        return
    18→    detail = step.get("detail", "")
    19→    if detail:
    20→        for line in detail.splitlines():
    21→            print(colorize_fn(f"         {line}", "dim"))
    22→    refs = step.get("issue_refs", [])
    23→    if refs:
    24→        print(colorize_fn(f"         Refs: {', '.join(refs)}", "dim"))
    25→
    26→
    27→__all__ = ["print_step"]
    28→
```

```
    18→
    19→def ensure_triage_started(plan: dict, services: TriageServices) -> dict:
    20→    """Auto-start triage if not started. Returns updated plan."""
    21→    if not has_triage_in_queue(plan):
    22→        inject_triage_stages(plan)
    23→        meta = plan.setdefault("epic_triage_meta", {})
    24→        meta.setdefault("triage_stages", {})
    25→        services.save_plan(plan)
    26→        print(colorize("  Planning mode auto-started.", "cyan"))
    27→    return plan
```

```
1→"""State readers for concern generation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections import defaultdict
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
     9→
    10→
    11→def _open_issues(state: StateModel) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    12→    """Return all open issues from state."""
    13→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    14→    return [
    15→        finding for finding in issues.values()
    16→        if isinstance(finding, dict) and finding.get("status") == "open"
    17→    ]
    18→
    19→
    20→def _group_by_file(state: StateModel) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]:
    21→    """Group open issues by file, excluding holistic (file='.') issues."""
    22→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
    23→    for finding in _open_issues(state):
    24→        file = finding.get("file", "")
    25→        if file and file != ".":
    26→            by_file[file].append(finding)
    27→    return dict(by_file)
    28→
    29→
    30→__all__ = ["_group_by_file", "_open_issues"]
    31→
```

```
1→"""Triage stage pipeline entrypoint — Codex subprocess runner and Claude orchestrator."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     8→
     9→from ..services import TriageServices
    10→from .orchestrator_claude import run_claude_orchestrator
    11→from .orchestrator_codex_pipeline import run_codex_pipeline
    12→from .orchestrator_common import STAGES
    13→
    14→
    15→def _parse_only_stages(raw: str | None) -> list[str]:
    16→    """Parse --only-stages comma-separated string into validated stage list."""
    17→    if not raw:
    18→        return list(STAGES)
    19→    stages = [s.strip().lower() for s in raw.split(",") if s.strip()]
    20→    for stage in stages:
    21→        if stage not in STAGES:
    22→            raise ValueError(f"Unknown stage: {stage!r}. Valid: {', '.join(STAGES)}")
    23→    return stages
    24→
    25→
    26→def do_run_triage_stages(
    27→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    28→    *,
    29→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
    30→) -> None:
    31→    """Run triage stages via the selected runner."""
    32→    runner = str(getattr(args, "runner", "codex")).strip().lower()
    33→
    34→    try:
    35→        stages_to_run = _parse_only_stages(getattr(args, "only_stages", None))
    36→    except ValueError as exc:
    37→        print(colorize(f"  {exc}", "red"))
    38→        return
    39→
    40→    if runner == "claude":
    41→        run_claude_orchestrator(args, services=services)
    42→    elif runner == "codex":
    43→        run_codex_pipeline(args, stages_to_run=stages_to_run, services=services)
    44→    else:
    45→        print(colorize(f"  Unknown runner: {runner}. Use 'codex' or 'claude'.", "red"))
    46→
    47→
    48→__all__ = ["_parse_only_stages", "do_run_triage_stages"]
    49→
```

```
1→"""Auto-complete action steps when their referenced issues leave the queue."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→def auto_complete_steps(plan: dict) -> list[str]:
     7→    """Mark steps done when all their issue_refs are no longer in the queue.
     8→
     9→    Returns list of human-readable messages for completed steps.
    10→    """
    11→    messages: list[str] = []
    12→    queue_set = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    13→
    14→    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    15→        for i, step in enumerate(cluster.get("action_steps") or []):
    16→            if not isinstance(step, dict) or step.get("done"):
    17→                continue
    18→            refs = step.get("issue_refs", [])
    19→            if not refs:
    20→                continue
    21→            # Match by suffix: ref "abc123" matches "review::path::abc123"
    22→            all_gone = all(
    23→                not any(qid.endswith(ref) or qid == ref for qid in queue_set)
    24→                for ref in refs
    25→            )
    26→            if all_gone:
    27→                step["done"] = True
    28→                messages.append(
    29→                    f"  Step {i + 1} of '{name}' auto-completed: {step.get('title', '')}"
    30→                )
    31→    return messages
    32→
    33→
    34→__all__ = ["auto_complete_steps"]
    35→
```

```
"""Wrapper, delegation, and facade pattern scanners."""

from __future__ import annotations

import ast

from desloppify.intelligence.review.context_holistic.budget_analysis import (
    _strip_docstring,
)


def _python_passthrough_target(stmt: ast.stmt) -> str | None:
    """Return passthrough call target when stmt is `return target(...)`."""
    if not isinstance(stmt, ast.Return):
        return None
    value = stmt.value
    if not isinstance(value, ast.Call):
        return None
    target = value.func
    if isinstance(target, ast.Name):
        return target.id
    return None


def _find_python_passthrough_wrappers(tree: ast.Module) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
    """Find Python wrapper pairs via AST traversal."""
    wrappers: list[tuple[str, str]] = []
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if not isinstance(node, ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef):
            continue

        body = _strip_docstring(list(node.body))
        if len(body) != 1:
            continue

        target_name = _python_passthrough_target(body[0])
        if target_name and node.name != target_name:
            wrappers.append((node.name, target_name))
    return wrappers


def _is_delegation_stmt(stmt: ast.stmt) -> str | None:
    """Return delegate attribute when *stmt* is a pure delegation call/access."""
    if isinstance(stmt, ast.Expr):
        value = stmt.value
    elif isinstance(stmt, ast.Return) and stmt.value is not None:
        value = stmt.value
    else:
        return None

    if isinstance(value, ast.Call):
        value = value.func

    node = value
    depth = 0
    while isinstance(node, ast.Attribute):
        node = node.value
        depth += 1
    if depth < 1 or not isinstance(node, ast.Name) or node.id != "self":
        return None

    first = value
    while isinstance(first, ast.Attribute) and isinstance(first.value, ast.Attribute):
        first = first.value
    if isinstance(first, ast.Attribute) and isinstance(first.value, ast.Name):
        return first.attr
    return None


def _find_delegation_heavy_classes(tree: ast.Module) -> list[dict]:
    """Find classes where most methods delegate to a single inner object."""
    results: list[dict] = []
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if not isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef):
            continue

        methods: list[ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef] = [
            child
            for child in node.body
            if isinstance(child, ast.FunctionDef | ast.AsyncFunctionDef)
```

```
"""Primitive drawing routines for scorecard left-panel rendering."""

from __future__ import annotations

from desloppify.app.output.scorecard_parts.theme import BG, BORDER, DIM, TEXT, scale, score_color


def draw_left_panel_version(
    draw,
    *,
    center_x: int,
    baseline_y: int,
    version_text: str,
    version_bbox,
    version_width: float,
    font_version,
) -> None:
    draw.text(
        (center_x - version_width / 2, baseline_y - version_bbox[1]),
        version_text,
        fill=DIM,
        font=font_version,
    )


def draw_left_panel_title(
    draw,
    *,
    center_x: int,
    title_y: int,
    title: str,
    title_bbox,
    title_width: float,
    font_title,
) -> None:
    draw.text(
        (center_x - title_width / 2, title_y - title_bbox[1]),
        title,
        fill=TEXT,
        font=font_title,
    )


def draw_left_panel_score(
    draw,
    *,
    center_x: int,
    score_y: int,
    score_text: str,
    score_bbox,
    score_value: float,
    font_big,
) -> None:
    score_width = draw.textlength(score_text, font=font_big)
    draw.text(
        (center_x - score_width / 2, score_y - score_bbox[1]),
        score_text,
        fill=score_color(score_value),
        font=font_big,
    )
```

```
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.config import DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
     8→
     9→from .render_support import scorecard_subjective as _scorecard_subjective_impl
    10→
    11→
    12→def _scorecard_subjective(state: dict, dim_scores: dict) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    13→    """Return scorecard-aligned subjective entries for current dimension scores."""
    14→    return _scorecard_subjective_impl(state, dim_scores)
    15→
    16→
    17→def _low_subjective_dimensions(
    18→    state: dict,
    19→    dim_scores: dict,
    20→    *,
    21→    threshold: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE,
    22→) -> list[tuple[str, float, int]]:
    23→    """Return assessed scorecard-subjective entries below the threshold."""
    24→    low: list[tuple[str, float, int]] = []
    25→    for entry in _scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores):
    26→        if entry.get("placeholder"):
    27→            continue
    28→        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
    29→        if strict_val < threshold:
    30→            low.append(
    31→                (
    32→                    str(entry.get("name", "Subjective")),
    33→                    strict_val,
    34→                    int(entry.get("failing", 0)),
    35→                )
    36→            )
    37→    low.sort(key=lambda item: item[1])
    38→    return low
    39→
    40→
    41→__all__ = ["_low_subjective_dimensions", "_scorecard_subjective"]
    42→
```

```
1→"""Observe-batch prompt builders for triage runner."""

     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→

     6→
     7→
     8→def _observe_batch_instructions(issue_count: int, total_batches: int) -> str:
     9→    return f"""\
    10→## OBSERVE Batch Instructions
    11→
    12→You are one of {total_batches} parallel observe batches. Your task: verify every issue
    13→assigned to you against the actual source code.
    14→
    15→**The review system has a high false-positive rate.** Issues frequently:
    16→- Claim "12 unsafe casts" when there are actually 2
    17→- Describe code that was already refactored
    18→- Propose over-engineering that would make things worse
    19→- Count props/returns/args wrong
    20→
    21→Your job is to catch these. A report that just restates issue titles is **worthless**.
    22→The value you add is reading the actual code and forming an independent judgment.
    23→
    24→Do NOT analyze themes, strategy, or relationships between issues. Just verify: is each issue real?
    25→
    26→**For EVERY issue you must:**
    27→- Open and read the actual source file
    28→- Verify specific claims: count the actual casts, props, returns, line count
    29→- Check if the suggested fix already exists (common false positive)
    30→- Report a clear verdict: genuine / false positive / exaggerated / over-engineering
    31→
    32→**What a GOOD report looks like:**
    33→- "[34580232] taskType is plain string — FALSE POSITIVE. Uses branded string union KnownTaskType
    34→  with ~25 literals in src/types/database.ts line 50. The issue describes code that doesn't exist."
    35→- "[b634fc71] useGenerationsPaneController returns 60+ values — GENUINE. Confirmed 65 properties
    36→  at lines 217-282. Mixes pane lifecycle, filters, gallery data, interaction, and navigation."
    37→
    38→**What a LAZY report looks like (will be rejected):**
    39→- "There are several convention issues that should be addressed"
    40→- "The type safety dimension has some genuine concerns"
```

```
1→"""Sense-check prompt builders for triage runner."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→
     8→def build_sense_check_content_prompt(
     9→    *,
    10→    cluster_name: str,
    11→    plan: dict,
    12→    repo_root: Path,
    13→) -> str:
    14→    """Build a content-verification prompt for a single cluster."""
    15→    cluster = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(cluster_name, {})
    16→    steps = cluster.get("action_steps", [])
    17→    issue_ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
    18→
    19→    parts: list[str] = []
    20→    parts.append(
    21→        f"You are sense-checking cluster `{cluster_name}` "
    22→        f"({len(steps)} steps, {len(issue_ids)} issues).\n"
    23→        f"Repo root: {repo_root}"
    24→    )
    25→
    26→    parts.append(
    27→        "## Your job\n"
    28→        "For EVERY step in this cluster, read the actual source file and verify\n"
    29→        "every factual claim. Then fix anything wrong or vague.\n"
    30→    )
    31→
    32→    parts.append(
    33→        "## What to check and fix\n"
    34→        "1. LINE NUMBERS: Does the code at the claimed lines match what the step describes?\n"
    35→        "   Fix: update the line range to match current file state.\n"
    36→        "2. NAMES: Do the function/variable/type names in the step exist in the file?\n"
    37→        "   Fix: correct the names.\n"
    38→        "3. COUNTS: \"Update the 3 imports\" — are there actually 3? Or 5?\n"
    39→        "   Fix: correct the count.\n"
    40→        "4. STALENESS: Is the problem the issue describes still present in the code?\n"
```

1→"""Static prompt text and stage-instruction helpers for triage runner."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→_STAGES = ("observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich", "sense-check")
     6→
     7→_PREAMBLE = """\
     8→You are a triage subagent with full codebase access and the desloppify CLI.
     9→Your job is to complete the **{stage}** stage of triage planning.
    10→
    11→Repo root: {repo_root}
    12→
    13→## Standards
    14→
    15→You are expected to produce **exceptional** work. The output of this triage becomes the
    16→actual plan that an executor follows — if you are lazy, vague, or sloppy, real work gets
    17→wasted. Concretely:
    18→
    19→- **Read the actual source code.** Every opinion you form must come from reading the file,
    20→  not from reading the issue title. Issues frequently exaggerate, miscount, or describe
    21→  code that has already been fixed. Trust nothing until you verify it.
    22→- **Have specific opinions.** "This seems like it could be an issue" is worthless. "This is
    23→  a false positive because line 47 already uses the pattern the issue suggests" is useful.
    24→- **Do the hard thinking.** If two issues seem related, figure out WHY. If something should
    25→  be skipped, explain the specific reason for THIS issue, not a generic category.
    26→- **Don't take shortcuts.** Reading 5 files and extrapolating to 30 is lazy. Read all 30.
    27→  If you have too many, use subagents to parallelize — don't skip.
    28→
    29→Use the desloppify CLI to record your work. Every command you run mutates plan.json directly.
    30→The orchestrator will review your work and confirm the stage after you record it.
    31→
    32→**CRITICAL: Only run commands for YOUR stage ({stage}).** Do NOT re-run earlier stages
    33→(e.g., do not run `--stage observe` if you are the organize subagent). Earlier stages
    34→are already confirmed. Re-running them will corrupt the plan state.
    35→"""
    36→
    37→_CLI_REFERENCE = """\
    38→## CLI Command Reference
    39→
    40→### Stage recording
    41→```
    42→desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "<analysis>"
    43→desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "<strategy>" --attestation "<80+ chars>"
    44→desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "<summary>" --attestation "<80+ chars>"
    45→desloppify plan triage --stage enrich --report "<enrichment summary>" --attestation "<80+ chars>"
    46→desloppify plan triage --stage sense-check --report "<verification summary>" --attestation "<80+ chars>"
    47→```
    48→
    49→### Cluster management
    50→```

## Summary: Smallest New Non-Test Files on Branch

Here are the **20 smallest new files** (lines of code):

1. **1 line**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/__init__.py` - Just a module docstring
2. **6 lines**: `desloppify/engine/_concerns/__init__.py` - Re-export facade
3. **7 lines**: `desloppify/engine/detectors/_flat_dirs/__init__.py` - Re-export facade
4. **9 lines**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/confirmations_advanced.py` - Re-export facade
5. **23 lines**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_ops.py` - Re-export facade
6. **25 lines**: `desloppify/engine/_concerns/constants.py` - Constants only
7. **27 lines**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_steps.py` - Single utility function
8. **27 lines**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator_common.py` - Shared helpers
9. **30 lines**: `desloppify/engine/_concerns/state.py` - Two utility functions
10. **32 lines**: `desloppify/languages/_framework/commands_base_scaffold.py` - Constants + two stub functions
11. **34 lines**: `desloppify/engine/_plan/step_completion.py` - One function
12. **36 lines**: `desloppify/engine/_concerns/utils.py` - Utility functions
13. **39 lines**: `desloppify/app/commands/next/options.py` - Single dataclass
14. **39 lines**: `desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/_stage_validation_completion.py` - Re-export facade
15. **41 lines**: `desloppify/app/commands/next/subjective.py` - Two wrapper functions
16. **41 lines**: `desloppify/engine/_concerns/types.py` - Type definitions
17. **44 lines**: `desloppify/languages/_framework/treesitter/_complexity_shared.py` - Shared helpers
18. **46 lines**: `desloppify/app/commands/next/render_workflow.py` - Implementation
19. **46 lines**: `desloppify/engine/_plan/sync_auto_prune.py` - Implementation
20. **47 lines**: `desloppify/languages/typescript/detectors/security_patterns.py` - Implementation

### Key Findings on Re-export Facades

**Three `__init__.py` files that are pure re-exports (and candidates for cleanup per CLAUDE.md policy):**

1. **`desloppify/engine/_concerns/__init__.py`** (6 lines) - Re-exports `Concern`, `cleanup_stale_dismissals`, `generate_concerns` from submodules
2. **`desloppify/engine/detectors/_flat_dirs/__init__.py`** (7 lines) - Re-exports `FlatDirDetectionConfig`, `detect_flat_dirs`, `format_flat_dir_summary` from submodules
3. **`desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/confirmations_advanced.py`** (9 lines) - Re-exports confirmation handlers from sibling modules
4. **`desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_ops.py`** (23 lines) - Re-exports all cluster operation commands from submodules (8 functions)
5. **`desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/_stage_validation_completion.py`** (39 lines) - Re-exports 17 validation functions from submodules

### Additionally Trivial:

- **`desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/__init__.py`** (1 line) - Just a module docstring, no actual code

- **`desloppify/languages/_framework/commands_base_scaffold.py`** (32 lines) - Constants and two **stub functions** (with `del` statements that discard parameters, returning empty dicts/lists)

- **`desloppify/app/commands/next/options.py`** (39 lines) - Single frozen dataclass with one classmethod

- **`desloppify/app/commands/next/subjective.py`** (41 lines) - Two thin wrapper functions that call a single implementation function

### Recommendation

According to the backward compatibility policy in CLAUDE.md ("remove re-export facades, wrapper shims, compat layers"), the following are candidates for consolidation:

- The five re-export files listed above could have their imports moved directly to their callers
- The wrapper files (`subjective.py`, the scaffold stubs) add minimal value if they're not actively used
- The 1-line `__init__.py` could be removed entirely if the module doesn't need a docstring
