## Test Quality Analysis: "Direct Coverage" Commits on `wip/triage-runner`

Based on my examination of the test files added in these commits, here's my findings:

### Summary

There are **77 "direct" test files** across the test suite, totaling **9,013 lines**. Of these, only **3 files** are purely re-export/wrapper tests. The vast majority test **meaningful, substantive behavior**.

---

### 1. **Are these tests meaningful or trivial?**

**Verdict: Mostly meaningful, with a small number of re-export tests**

The test coverage falls into clear categories:

- **~70+ files**: Test actual algorithm behavior, data structure transformations, and meaningful business logic
- **~3 files**: Test re-export facades (budget_abstractions, budget_analysis, budget_patterns_wrappers)

Even the re-export tests include functional assertions beyond just "does the symbol exist":

---

### 2. **Specific Test Files Examined**

#### **test_context_holistic_budget_abstractions_direct.py** (77 lines)
- **What it tests**: Re-export module that consolidates abstraction-economy helpers
- **Quality**: Mix of trivial and substantive
  - `test_budget_abstractions_exports_expected_symbols()` — trivial, just checks `__all__`
  - `test_budget_abstractions_re_exports_reference_source_modules()` — trivial, verifies identity with `is`
  - `test_budget_abstractions_compute_sub_axes_callable_via_re_export()` — **substantive**: calls the re-exported function with realistic parameters and validates output structure (6 expected sub-axes)

#### **test_context_holistic_budget_patterns_wrappers_direct.py** (63 lines)
- **What it tests**: AST-based wrapper/delegation pattern detection
- **Quality**: Excellent. Tests meaningful algorithmic behavior
  - `test_python_passthrough_target_detects_return_call_name()` — actual AST parsing, semantic detection
  - `test_find_python_passthrough_wrappers_reports_wrapper_pairs()` — detects wrapper/target pairs
  - `test_find_facade_modules_detects_re_export_heavy_module()` — calculates re-export ratios, validates thresholds

#### **test_context_holistic_clusters_security_direct.py** (92 lines)
- **What it tests**: Cluster builder logic for security hotspots
- **Quality**: Excellent. Tests complex data aggregation and filtering
  - Tests apply severity-based filtering, file-level aggregation, sorting order
  - Tests signal density counting with detector deduplication
  - Tests systemic pattern detection with file thresholds and hotspot ranking
  - All tests use realistic issue payloads and validate multi-field output structures

#### **test_context_holistic_clusters_error_state_direct.py** (67 lines)
- **What it tests**: Error hotspot filtering and mutable globals grouping
- **Quality: Excellent. Tests thresholding and aggregation logic
  - `test_build_error_hotspots_filters_threshold_sorts_and_limits()` — validates threshold enforcement (keeps top 20), verifies sorting order, checks field mapping
  - `test_build_mutable_globals_groups_names_and_uses_default_mutation_weight()` — aggregates by file, groups names, sums mutations

#### **test_scorecard_left_panel_primitives_direct.py** (147 lines)
- **What it tests**: Drawing primitives for PIL Image rendering
- **Quality: Excellent. Tests geometric calculations and state management
  - Tests coordinate centering logic: `(center_x - width / 2, baseline_y - bbox[1])`
  - Tests monkeypatching of color/scale functions and verifies call capture
  - Tests complex multi-element layouts (label + value with gap calculations)
  - Tests box rendering with radius and styling

---

### 3. **Test Quality Patterns**

| Test Category | Count | Quality Level | Example |
|---|---|---|---|
| Algorithm/Logic Tests | 60+ | High | Holistic cluster builders, AST pattern detection, aggregation logic |
| Drawing/Rendering Tests | 8+ | High | Scorecard primitives, coordinate calculations, styling |
| Data Transformation Tests | 8+ | High | Work queue ranking, item section rendering, projection helpers |
| Re-export Facade Tests | 3 | Mixed | budget_abstractions (half trivial, half functional) |

---

### 4. **Re-export Facade Tests Are Defensible**

The 3 re-export tests exist for modules that organize related functions (e.g., `budget_abstractions.py` consolidates 9 functions from 2 source modules). These tests:
- **Verify the API contract**: What's exported is consistent
- **Catch breaking changes**: If a developer removes or renames a re-export, tests fail
- **Include at least one functional call** to validate the re-export path works end-to-end

---

### 5. **Line Counts**
```

budget_patterns_wrappers_direct.py      : 63 lines (excellent)

plan_render_items_direct.py              : 82 lines (excellent)
                                         ──────────
All 77 "direct" test files             : 9,013 lines total
```

---

### Conclusion

**These are NOT trivial tests.** Only 3 out of 77 files are re-export tests, and even those include functional assertions. The bulk of the test suite validates:
- Complex data aggregation and filtering logic
- AST-based pattern detection algorithms
- Geometric/rendering calculations
- State transformations with multiple fields

The "direct coverage" commit strategy is working well: each commit adds focused tests for a specific module's actual behavior, not just import plumbing.

```
2. PLAN OVERRIDE SPLIT (override_io.py, override_misc.py, override_resolve_cmd.py,

COUPLING ANALYSIS:
- override_handlers.py acts as a facade with wrappers around each module
  (lines 68-130 = mostly just delegation via _*_impl aliases)
- Each override_* module IMPORTS override_handlers at runtime:
    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
- This creates circular dependency:
    override_handlers imports from override_* modules
    override_* modules import override_handlers back
- override_resolve_cmd imports from override_resolve_helpers (only cross-file dependency within command logic)

CODE REUSE PATTERN:
- All override modules follow identical pattern:

    2. Import override_handlers at runtime to access shared state/helpers
    3. Call override_handlers.* functions for core logic
- override_misc.py has 5 command functions (describe, note, reopen, focus, scan-gate)
- override_skip.py has 2 command functions (skip, unskip)
- override_resolve_cmd.py has 1 large command function (resolve)

ASSESSMENT: QUESTIONABLE SPLIT
- Split by command feature, not by shared concern
- Each file is largely self-contained but thin (no heavy imports or shared helpers between them)
- override_io.py is legitimate (transactional I/O is a distinct concern)
- override_resolve_helpers.py is legitimate (3 cohesive helpers for resolve logic)
- But override_misc.py groups 5 unrelated commands; could be back in override_handlers
- Circular import pattern suggests incomplete separation
- override_handlers.py becomes a large wrapper facade (141 lines) with 34+ function aliases

VERDICT: Partial fragmentation. The split makes sense for I/O and resolve helpers, 
but override_misc.py spreads thin functionality across multiple modules.

  signals.py                          96 lines (signal extraction & classification)
  text.py                            203 lines (summary/question/evidence builders)
  generators.py                      242 lines (main concern generation logic)

TOTAL: ~679 lines across 8 files

COHESION ANALYSIS:
- constants.py: Trivial constants re-exported from detectors (MIN_DETECTORS_FOR_MIXED, thresholds)
- types.py: Pure data types (Concern, ConcernSignals, SignalKey)
- utils.py: Self-contained utilities (fingerprint, dismissal checking)
- state.py: Data access layer (filtering/grouping issues)
- signals.py: Signal extraction & quantitative analysis
- text.py: Narrative construction (summaries, questions, evidence)
- generators.py: Orchestrates the whole pipeline

IMPORT FLOW:
  generators.py → imports from all other modules
  text.py → imports from constants, types, utils
  signals.py → imports from constants, types, utils
  state.py → no internal imports
  types.py → no internal imports
  utils.py → imports from types
  constants.py → imports from external detectors only

FACADE (concerns.py):
- Exports only 2 public functions: generate_concerns, cleanup_stale_dismissals
- Re-exports Concern type
- Everything else (underscore-prefixed) is internal
- Well-scoped facade (3 public exports, rest internal)

ASSESSMENT: WELL-STRUCTURED SPLIT
- Separation of concerns: data, signals, narrative, generation
- Each module has a single responsibility
- No circular imports; clean dependency graph
- generators.py is the orchestrator (242 lines, reasonable size)
- Constants module is minimal but justified (keeps thresholds distinct)
- Supports testing individual signal/text generation without full pipeline

VERDICT: This is a HIGH-QUALITY split. Each module serves a distinct purpose,
and the facade properly separates internal details from public API.

=================================================

FILES & SIZES:
  _stage_validation.py                293 lines (main validation dispatcher)
  _stage_validation_completion.py      39 lines (RE-EXPORT FACADE ONLY)

  Lines 22-39: __all__ re-exports

IMPORT STRUCTURE:

  _stage_validation_completion.py imports from:

    - _stage_validation_completion_stages

This creates unnecessary indirection:
```

## Summary

Based on a thorough examination of the recent split module refactors, here's my assessment:

### 1. **Triage Runner Split** (`desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/`)
**Verdict: GOOD** ✓

- 13 files, ~1,361 total lines
- Clear functional separation: Codex orchestrators, Claude orchestrator, prompt builders
- Each `stage_prompts_*` module handles distinct stage logic (observe, sense, sense-check)
- No trivial re-export wrappers; every file contains substantive code
- File sizes reasonable (27–373 lines, average ~105)
- Clean import patterns; well-managed dependencies

### 2. **Plan Override Split** (`override_*.py` files)
**Verdict: PARTIAL FRAGMENTATION** ⚠

- 5 files, ~862 total lines
- **Legitimate parts:**
  - `override_io.py` (71 lines): Transactional I/O is a coherent concern
  - `override_resolve_helpers.py` (105 lines): 3 cohesive helpers for resolve-specific logic

- **Problematic aspects:**
  - `override_misc.py` (210 lines): Groups 5 *unrelated* commands (describe, note, reopen, focus, scan-gate) — these could be back in `override_handlers`
  - **Circular imports**: Each override module does `from . import override_handlers as host` while `override_handlers` imports from them. This is a code smell indicating incomplete separation.
  - `override_handlers.py` becomes a 141-line wrapper facade with 34+ function aliases, defeating the purpose of the split

### 3. **_concerns Package** (`desloppify/engine/_concerns/`)
**Verdict: EXCELLENT** ✓✓

- 8 files, ~679 total lines
- Clear separation of concerns: data types → state readers → signal extraction → narrative building → generation orchestration
- Clean dependency graph (no circular imports):
  - `generators.py` orchestrates; imports from all others
  - `text.py` & `signals.py` import from `constants`, `types`, `utils`
  - Leaf modules (`types.py`, `state.py`) have no internal imports
- Well-scoped facade (`concerns.py`): exports only 2 public functions + 1 type
- Each module has a single, clear responsibility
- File sizes are reasonable (25–242 lines)

### 4. **Stage Validation Split** (`_stage_validation*.py`)
**Verdict: FRAGMENTED WITH UNNECESSARY WRAPPER** ✗

- 5 files, ~947 total lines
- **The problem:** `_stage_validation_completion.py` (39 lines) is a **pure re-export wrapper**:
  - Contains no logic, only imports and `__all__` exports
  - Creates unnecessary indirection: `_stage_validation.py` → `_completion.py` → `_completion_policy.py`
- **Legitimate submodules:**
  - `_completion_policy.py` (179 lines): Strategy/cluster/note validation — cohesive
  - `_completion_stages.py` (189 lines): Per-stage auto-confirm logic — cohesive
  - `_enrich_checks.py` (247 lines): Cluster/step validation rules — cohesive

- **Recommendation:** Eliminate `_stage_validation_completion.py`. Either import directly from the policy/stages modules, or merge them into a single `_stage_validation_completion.py` (they're closely related and always imported together).

### 5. **Trivial Re-exports**
Found one clear anti-pattern:

| File | Size | Issue |
|------|------|-------|
| `_stage_validation_completion.py` | 39 lines | Pure re-export facade; adds layer without value |
| `orchestrator_common.py` | 27 lines | **Justified** — prevents circular imports between orchestrators |
| `concerns/_concerns/constants.py` | 25 lines | **Justified** — centralizes magic numbers for testing |

---

### Overall Verdict

**70% successful refactoring:**

**Good splits** that improve architecture:
- ✓ `triage/runner/` — Well-designed, functional boundaries make sense
- ✓ `_concerns/` — Exemplary separation of concerns
- ✓ `override_io.py`, `override_resolve_helpers.py` — Legitimate distinct modules

**Overdone splits** that just spread code thin:
- ⚠ `override_misc.py` — 5 unrelated commands; violates single-responsibility
- ⚠ Circular imports in `override_*` — Suggests incomplete separation

**Definite fragmentation smell:**
- ✗ `_stage_validation_completion.py` — Unnecessary wrapper layer

The refactors show good intent but uneven execution. The triage/runner and _concerns splits are architectural wins. The override split creates more complexity than it solves (circular imports, facade overhead). The stage_validation split is mostly sound except for one unnecessary indirection layer.

```
    24→    issue_ids = host.resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)

    27→        return
    28→

    30→        host.describe_issue(plan, fid, text or None)
```

```
1→"""Helper logic for plan resolve command workflow/triage gating."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     6→
     7→
     8→def check_cluster_guard(patterns: list[str], plan: dict, state: dict) -> bool:
     9→    """Return True if blocked by cluster guard, False if OK to proceed."""
    10→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    11→
    12→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    13→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    14→    for pattern in patterns:
    15→        if pattern in clusters:
    16→            cluster = clusters[pattern]
    17→            ids = [
    18→                fid
    19→                for fid in cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
    20→                if fid in issues and issues[fid].get("status") == "open"
    21→            ]
    22→            if len(ids) == 0:
    23→                print(
    24→                    colorize(
    25→                        f"\n  Cluster '{pattern}' is empty — add items before marking it done.\n",
    26→                        "yellow",
    27→                    )
    28→                )
    29→                print(colorize(f"  Use: desloppify plan cluster add {pattern} <issue-id>", "dim"))
    30→                return True
```

```
1→"""I/O and transactional persistence helpers for plan override commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
     9→from desloppify.engine.plan import get_plan_file, plan_path_for_state
    10→
    11→
    12→def _resolve_state_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    13→    return path if path is not None else state_mod.get_state_file()
    14→
    15→
    16→def _resolve_plan_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    17→    return path if path is not None else get_plan_file()
    18→
    19→
    20→def _plan_file_for_state(state_file: Path | None) -> Path | None:
    21→    if state_file is None:
    22→        return None
    23→    return plan_path_for_state(state_file)
    24→
    25→
    26→def _snapshot_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    27→    if not path.exists():
    28→        return None
    29→    return path.read_text()
    30→
    31→
    32→def _restore_file_snapshot(path: Path, snapshot: str | None) -> None:
    33→    if snapshot is None:
    34→        try:
    35→            path.unlink()
    36→        except FileNotFoundError:
    37→            return
    38→        return
    39→    safe_write_text(path, snapshot)
    40→
    41→
    42→def save_plan_state_transactional(
    43→    *,
    44→    plan: dict,
    45→    plan_path: Path | None,
    46→    state_data: dict,
    47→    state_path_value: Path | None,
    48→) -> None:
    49→    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
    50→    effective_plan_path = _resolve_plan_file(plan_path)
    51→    effective_state_path = _resolve_state_file(state_path_value)
    52→    plan_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_plan_path)
    53→    state_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_state_path)
    54→
    55→    try:
    56→        state_mod.save_state(state_data, effective_state_path)
    57→        # Keep this import-time indirection so tests monkeypatching
    58→        # override_handlers.save_plan still affect transactional writes.
    59→        from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    60→
    61→        host.save_plan(plan, effective_plan_path)
    62→    except Exception:
    63→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_state_path, state_snapshot)
    64→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_plan_path, plan_snapshot)
    65→        raise
    66→
    67→
    68→__all__ = [
    69→    "_plan_file_for_state",
    70→    "save_plan_state_transactional",
    71→]
    72→
```

```
    60→def cmd_plan_reopen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:

    62→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415

    64→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])

   147→def cmd_plan_scan_gate(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   148→    """Check or skip the scan requirement for workflow items."""
   149→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
   150→
   151→    skip = getattr(args, "skip", False)
   152→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   153→
   154→    plan = host.load_plan()
   155→    scan_count_at_start = plan.get("scan_count_at_plan_start")
   156→
   157→    if scan_count_at_start is None:
   158→        print(colorize("  No active plan cycle (plan_start_scores not seeded).", "dim"))
   159→        print(colorize("  Scan gate is not applicable — workflow items gate themselves.", "dim"))
   160→        return
   161→
   162→    resolved_state_path = host.state_path(args)
   163→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(resolved_state_path)
   164→    current_scan_count = int(state_data.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   165→    scan_ran = current_scan_count > scan_count_at_start
   166→    scan_skipped = plan.get("scan_gate_skipped", False)
   167→
   168→    if not skip:
   169→        if scan_ran:
   170→            print(colorize("  Scan gate: PASSED", "green"))
   171→            print(colorize(f"  Scans at cycle start: {scan_count_at_start}  Current: {current_scan_count}", "dim"))
   172→        elif scan_skipped:
   173→            print(colorize("  Scan gate: SKIPPED (manually)", "yellow"))
   174→        else:
   175→            print(colorize("  Scan gate: BLOCKED", "red"))
   176→            print(colorize(f"  Scans at cycle start: {scan_count_at_start}  Current: {current_scan_count}", "dim"))
   177→            print(colorize("  Run: desloppify scan", "dim"))
   178→            print(colorize('  Or:  desloppify plan scan-gate --skip --note "reason"', "dim"))
   179→        return
   180→
   181→    if scan_ran:
   182→        print(colorize("  Scan already ran this cycle — no skip needed.", "green"))
   183→        return
   184→
   185→    if not note or len(note.strip()) < 50:
   186→        print(colorize("  --skip requires --note with at least 50 chars explaining why.", "red"))
   187→        return
   188→
   189→    plan["scan_gate_skipped"] = True
   190→    host.append_log_entry(
   191→        plan,
   192→        "scan_gate_skip",
   193→        actor="user",
   194→        note=note,
   195→        detail={
   196→            "scan_count_at_start": scan_count_at_start,
   197→            "current_scan_count": current_scan_count,
   198→        },
   199→    )
   200→    host.save_plan(plan)
   201→    print(colorize("  Scan requirement marked as satisfied (logged).", "yellow"))
   202→
   203→

   208→    "cmd_plan_reopen",

   210→]
```

```
1→"""Helper logic for plan resolve command workflow/triage gating."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     6→
     7→
     8→def check_cluster_guard(patterns: list[str], plan: dict, state: dict) -> bool:
     9→    """Return True if blocked by cluster guard, False if OK to proceed."""
    10→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    11→
    12→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    13→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    14→    for pattern in patterns:
    15→        if pattern in clusters:
    16→            cluster = clusters[pattern]
    17→            ids = [
    18→                fid
    19→                for fid in cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
    20→                if fid in issues and issues[fid].get("status") == "open"
    21→            ]
    22→            if len(ids) == 0:
    23→                print(
    24→                    colorize(
    25→                        f"\n  Cluster '{pattern}' is empty — add items before marking it done.\n",
    26→                        "yellow",
    27→                    )
    28→                )
    29→                print(colorize(f"  Use: desloppify plan cluster add {pattern} <issue-id>", "dim"))
    30→                return True
    31→            if len(ids) <= host._CLUSTER_INDIVIDUAL_THRESHOLD:
    32→                print_cluster_guard(pattern, ids, state)
    33→                return True
    34→    return False
    35→
    36→
    37→def print_cluster_guard(cluster_name: str, issue_ids: list[str], state: dict) -> None:
    38→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    39→    print(
    40→        colorize(
    41→            f"\n  Cluster '{cluster_name}' has {len(issue_ids)} item(s) — mark them done individually first:\n",
    42→            "yellow",
    43→        )
    44→    )
    45→    for fid in issue_ids:
    46→        issue = issues.get(fid, {})
    47→        summary = issue.get("summary", "(no summary)")[:80]
    48→        detector = issue.get("detector", "?")
    49→        print(f"    {fid}  [{detector}]  {summary}")
    50→    print(
    51→        colorize(
    52→            "\n  Use: desloppify resolve <id> --status fixed --note '...' --attest '...'",
    53→            "dim",
    54→        )
    55→    )
    56→    print(
    57→        colorize(
    58→            "  Or mark each resolved: desloppify plan resolve <id> --note '...' --confirm\n",
    59→            "dim",
    60→        )
    61→    )
    62→
    63→
    64→def is_synthetic_id(fid: str) -> bool:
    65→    """Return True if the ID is a synthetic workflow/triage item."""
    66→    return fid.startswith("triage::") or fid.startswith("workflow::") or fid.startswith("subjective::")
    67→
    68→
    69→def resolve_synthetic_ids(patterns: list[str]) -> tuple[list[str], list[str]]:
    70→    """Separate synthetic IDs from real issue patterns."""
    71→    synthetic = [pattern for pattern in patterns if is_synthetic_id(pattern)]
    72→    remaining = [pattern for pattern in patterns if not is_synthetic_id(pattern)]
    73→    return synthetic, remaining
    74→
    75→
    76→def blocked_triage_stages(plan: dict) -> dict[str, list[str]]:
    77→    """Return triage stages that are blocked by unmet dependencies."""
    78→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    79→
    80→    order_set = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    81→    present = order_set & host.TRIAGE_IDS
    82→    if not present:
    83→        return {}
    84→
    85→    confirmed = set(plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {}).get("triage_stages", {}).keys())
    86→    stage_names = ("observe", "reflect", "organize", "enrich", "sense-check", "commit")
    87→
    88→    blocked: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    89→    for sid, name in zip(host.TRIAGE_STAGE_IDS, stage_names, strict=False):
    90→        if sid not in present or name in confirmed:
    91→            continue
    92→        deps = host.TRIAGE_STAGE_DEPENDENCIES.get(name, set())
    93→        unmet = sorted(
    94→            f"triage::{dep}" for dep in deps if f"triage::{dep}" in present and dep not in confirmed
    95→        )
    96→        if unmet:
    97→            blocked[sid] = unmet
    98→    return blocked
    99→
   100→
```

```
1→"""Skip and unskip command handlers for plan overrides."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations

     6→import sys

     8→
     9→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    10→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    11→from desloppify.base.output.user_message import print_user_message
    12→
    13→
    14→def _validate_skip_requirements(
    15→    *,
    16→    kind: str,
    17→    attestation: str | None,
    18→    note: str | None,
    19→) -> bool:
    20→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    21→
    22→    if not host.skip_kind_requires_attestation(kind):
    23→        return True
    24→    if not host.validate_attestation(attestation):
    25→        host.show_attestation_requirement(
    26→            "Permanent skip" if kind == "permanent" else "False positive",
    27→            attestation,
    28→            host.ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
    29→        )
    30→        return False
    31→    if host.skip_kind_requires_note(kind) and not note:
    32→        print(
    33→            colorize("  --permanent requires --note to explain the decision.", "yellow"),
    34→            file=sys.stderr,
    35→        )
    36→        return False
    37→    return True
    38→
    39→
    40→def _apply_state_skip_resolution(
    41→    *,
    42→    kind: str,
    43→    state_file: Path | None,
    44→    issue_ids: list[str],
    45→    note: str | None,
    46→    attestation: str | None,
    47→) -> dict | None:
    48→    status = _skip_kind_state_status(kind)
    49→    if status is None:
    50→        return None
    51→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    52→    for fid in issue_ids:
    53→        state_mod.resolve_issues(
    54→            state_data,
    55→            fid,
    56→            status,
    57→            note or "",
    58→            attestation=attestation,
    59→        )
    60→    return state_data
    61→
    62→
    63→def _skip_kind_state_status(kind: str) -> str | None:
    64→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    65→
    66→    return host.skip_kind_state_status(kind)
    67→
    68→
    69→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    70→    """Skip issues — unified command for temporary/permanent/false-positive."""
    71→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    72→
    73→    runtime = host.command_runtime(args)
    74→    state = runtime.state
    75→    if not host.require_completed_scan(state):
    76→        return
    77→
    78→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    79→    reason: str | None = getattr(args, "reason", None)
    80→    review_after: int | None = getattr(args, "review_after", None)
```

Excellent! Now I have the full picture. Let me create a comprehensive analysis document.

## Import Circular Analysis - Override Modules

I've examined the import sections of all six override modules. Here's what I found:

### Import Chain Structure

**override_handlers.py** (the hub):
- **Lines 49-60**: Imports from split-out modules at module level:
  - `override_io`: `_plan_file_for_state`, `save_plan_state_transactional`
  - `override_misc`: 5 command implementations
  - `override_resolve_cmd`: `cmd_plan_resolve`
  - `override_resolve_helpers`: 2 helper functions
  - `override_skip`: 2 command implementations

### No Circular Imports Found ✓

**Key safety measures in place:**

1. **override_io.py** (line 59): Uses **deferred import** inside function `save_plan_state_transactional()`:
   ```python
   from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
   ```
   This is intentional—it only imports at runtime when called, not at module load time.

2. **override_misc.py**: All 5 functions (`cmd_plan_describe`, `cmd_plan_note`, `cmd_plan_reopen`, `cmd_plan_focus`, `cmd_plan_scan_gate`) use **deferred imports** inside their bodies:
   ```python
   from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
   ```

3. **override_resolve_cmd.py** (line 18): Deferred import in `cmd_plan_resolve()`:
   ```python
   from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
   ```

4. **override_resolve_helpers.py**: Two functions use deferred imports:
   - `check_cluster_guard()` (line 10): deferred import
   - `blocked_triage_stages()` (line 78): deferred import

5. **override_skip.py**: Multiple functions use deferred imports:
   - `_validate_skip_requirements()` (line 20)
   - `_skip_kind_state_status()` (line 64)
   - `cmd_plan_skip()` (line 71)
   - `cmd_plan_unskip()` (implied, not shown in excerpt)

### Summary

**No circular import issues detected.** The split-out modules:
- Do NOT statically import from `override_handlers`
- Use deferred (`# noqa: PLC0415`) imports inside function bodies only
- `override_handlers` safely imports from all split modules at the top level

This is the correct pattern for a refactored module that's been split: the hub module aggregates the imports, while leaf modules defer their access back to the hub until runtime.

```
    27→from .cluster_ops_manage import _cmd_cluster_create
    28→from .cluster_ops_manage import _cmd_cluster_delete
    29→from .cluster_ops_manage import _cmd_cluster_export
    30→from .cluster_ops_manage import _cmd_cluster_import
    31→from .cluster_ops_manage import _cmd_cluster_merge
    32→from .cluster_ops_reorder import _cmd_cluster_reorder
    33→from .cluster_update import cmd_cluster_update as _cmd_cluster_update_impl
    34→
    35→_HEX8_RE = re.compile(r"^[0-9a-f]{8}$")
    36→
    37→
    38→def _suggest_close_matches(state: dict, plan: dict | None, patterns: list[str]) -> None:
    39→    """Print fuzzy match suggestions for patterns that resolved to zero issues."""
    40→    all_ids: list[str] = list(state.get("issues", {}).keys())
    41→    if plan is not None:
    42→        seen_ids: set[str] = set(all_ids)
    43→        for fid in plan.get("queue_order", []):
    44→            if fid not in seen_ids:
    45→                seen_ids.add(fid)
    46→                all_ids.append(fid)
    47→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    48→            for fid in cluster.get("issue_ids", []):
    49→                if fid not in seen_ids:
    50→                    seen_ids.add(fid)
    51→                    all_ids.append(fid)
    52→
    53→    for pattern in patterns:
    54→        segments = pattern.split("::")
    55→        last_seg = segments[-1]
    56→        suggestions: list[str] = []
    57→
    58→        if _HEX8_RE.match(last_seg):
    59→            suggestions = [fid for fid in all_ids if fid.endswith(f"::{last_seg}") or fid == last_seg]
    60→            if suggestions:
    61→                print(colorize(f"  No match for: {pattern!r}", "yellow"))
    62→                print(colorize("  Did you mean:", "dim"))
    63→                for match in suggestions[:3]:
    64→                    print(colorize(f"    {match}", "dim"))
    65→                print(colorize(f"  Tip: match by hash suffix alone: {last_seg}", "dim"))
    66→            continue
    67→
    68→        slug = segments[-2] if len(segments) >= 2 else ""
    69→        for fid in all_ids:
    70→            if f"::{last_seg}::" in fid or fid.endswith(f"::{last_seg}"):
    71→                suggestions.append(fid)
    72→            elif slug and (f"::{slug}::" in fid or fid.endswith(f"::{slug}")):
    73→                suggestions.append(fid)
    74→        if suggestions:
    75→            print(colorize(f"  No match for: {pattern!r}", "yellow"))
    76→            print(colorize("  Did you mean:", "dim"))
    77→            for match in suggestions[:3]:
    78→                print(colorize(f"    {match}", "dim"))
    79→
    80→
    81→def _print_pattern_hints() -> None:
    82→    """Print valid pattern format hints after a no-match error."""
    83→    print(colorize("  Valid patterns:", "dim"))
    84→    print(colorize("    f41b3eb7              (8-char hash suffix from dashboard)", "dim"))
    85→    print(colorize("    review::path::name    (ID prefix)", "dim"))
    86→    print(colorize("    review                (all issues from detector)", "dim"))
    87→    print(colorize("    src/foo.py            (all issues in file)", "dim"))
    88→    print(colorize("    timing_attack         (issue name — last ::segment of ID)", "dim"))
    89→    print(colorize("    review::*naming*      (glob pattern)", "dim"))
    90→    print(colorize("    my-cluster            (cluster name — expands to members)", "dim"))
    91→
    92→
    93→def _cmd_cluster_add(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    94→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    95→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    96→        return
    97→
    98→    cluster_name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
    99→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   100→    dry_run: bool = getattr(args, "dry_run", False)
   101→
   102→    plan = load_plan()
   103→    issue_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   104→    if not issue_ids:
   105→        print(colorize("  No matching issues found.", "yellow"))
   106→        _print_pattern_hints()
   107→        _suggest_close_matches(state, plan, patterns)
   108→        return
   109→
   110→    if dry_run:
   111→        print(colorize(f"  [dry-run] Would add {len(issue_ids)} item(s) to {cluster_name}:", "cyan"))
   112→        for fid in issue_ids:
   113→            print(colorize(f"    {fid}", "dim"))
   114→        return
   115→
   116→    try:
   117→        count = add_to_cluster(plan, cluster_name, issue_ids)
   118→    except ValueError as ex:
   119→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   120→        return
   121→
   122→    member_set = set(issue_ids)
   123→    for other_name, other_cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
   124→        if other_name == cluster_name or other_cluster.get("auto"):
   125→            continue
   126→        other_ids = set(other_cluster.get("issue_ids", []))
   127→        if not other_ids:
   128→            continue
   129→        overlap = member_set & other_ids
   130→        if len(overlap) > len(other_ids) * 0.5:
   131→            percent = int(len(overlap) / len(other_ids) * 100)
   132→            print(
   133→                colorize(
   134→                    f"  Warning: {len(overlap)} issue(s) also in cluster '{other_name}' "
   135→                    f"({len(overlap)}/{len(other_ids)} = {percent}% overlap).",
   136→                    "yellow",
   137→                )
   138→            )
   139→
   140→    append_log_entry(plan, "cluster_add", issue_ids=issue_ids, cluster_name=cluster_name, actor="user")
   141→    save_plan(plan)
   142→    print(colorize(f"  Added {count} item(s) to cluster {cluster_name}.", "green"))
   143→
   144→
   145→def _cmd_cluster_remove(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   146→    state = command_runtime(args).state
   147→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   148→        return
   149→
   150→    cluster_name: str = getattr(args, "cluster_name", "")
   151→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   152→    dry_run: bool = getattr(args, "dry_run", False)
   153→
   154→    plan = load_plan()
   155→    issue_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   156→    if not issue_ids:
   157→        print(colorize("  No matching issues found.", "yellow"))
   158→        _print_pattern_hints()
   159→        _suggest_close_matches(state, plan, patterns)
   160→        return
   161→
   162→    if dry_run:
   163→        print(colorize(f"  [dry-run] Would remove {len(issue_ids)} item(s) from {cluster_name}:", "cyan"))
   164→        for fid in issue_ids:
   165→            print(colorize(f"    {fid}", "dim"))
   166→        return
   167→
   168→    try:
   169→        count = remove_from_cluster(plan, cluster_name, issue_ids)
   170→    except ValueError as ex:
   171→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   172→        return
   173→
   174→    append_log_entry(plan, "cluster_remove", issue_ids=issue_ids, cluster_name=cluster_name, actor="user")
   175→    save_plan(plan)
   176→    print(colorize(f"  Removed {count} item(s) from cluster {cluster_name}.", "green"))
   177→
   178→
   179→def _cmd_cluster_update(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   180→    """Update cluster description, steps, and/or priority."""
   181→    _cmd_cluster_update_impl(
   182→        args,
   183→        load_plan_fn=load_plan,
   184→        save_plan_fn=save_plan,
   185→        append_log_entry_fn=append_log_entry,
   186→        plan_lock_fn=plan_lock,
   187→        parse_steps_file_fn=parse_steps_file,
   188→        normalize_step_fn=normalize_step,
   189→        step_summary_fn=step_summary,
   190→        utc_now_fn=utc_now,
   191→        colorize_fn=colorize,
   192→    )
   193→
   194→
   195→def cmd_cluster_dispatch(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   196→    """Route cluster subcommands."""
   197→    cluster_action = getattr(args, "cluster_action", None)
   198→    dispatch = {
   199→        "create": _cmd_cluster_create,
   200→        "add": _cmd_cluster_add,
   201→        "remove": _cmd_cluster_remove,
   202→        "delete": _cmd_cluster_delete,
   203→        "reorder": _cmd_cluster_reorder,
   204→        "show": _cmd_cluster_show,
   205→        "list": _cmd_cluster_list,
   206→        "update": _cmd_cluster_update,
   207→        "merge": _cmd_cluster_merge,
   208→        "export": _cmd_cluster_export,
   209→        "import": _cmd_cluster_import,
   210→    }
   211→    handler = dispatch.get(cluster_action)
   212→    if handler is None:
   213→        _cmd_cluster_list(args)
   214→        return
   215→    handler(args)
   216→
   217→
   218→__all__ = ["_cmd_cluster_add", "_cmd_cluster_remove", "_cmd_cluster_update", "cmd_cluster_dispatch"]
   219→
```

```
1→"""Triage stage pipeline entrypoint — Codex subprocess runner and Claude orchestrator."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     8→
     9→from ..services import TriageServices
    10→from .orchestrator_claude import run_claude_orchestrator
    11→from .orchestrator_codex_pipeline import run_codex_pipeline
    12→from .orchestrator_common import STAGES
    13→
    14→
    15→def _parse_only_stages(raw: str | None) -> list[str]:
    16→    """Parse --only-stages comma-separated string into validated stage list."""
    17→    if not raw:
    18→        return list(STAGES)
    19→    stages = [s.strip().lower() for s in raw.split(",") if s.strip()]
    20→    for stage in stages:
    21→        if stage not in STAGES:
    22→            raise ValueError(f"Unknown stage: {stage!r}. Valid: {', '.join(STAGES)}")
    23→    return stages
    24→
    25→
    26→def do_run_triage_stages(
    27→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    28→    *,
    29→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
    30→) -> None:
    31→    """Run triage stages via the selected runner."""
    32→    runner = str(getattr(args, "runner", "codex")).strip().lower()
    33→
    34→    try:
    35→        stages_to_run = _parse_only_stages(getattr(args, "only_stages", None))
    36→    except ValueError as exc:
    37→        print(colorize(f"  {exc}", "red"))
    38→        return
    39→
    40→    if runner == "claude":
    41→        run_claude_orchestrator(args, services=services)
    42→    elif runner == "codex":
    43→        run_codex_pipeline(args, stages_to_run=stages_to_run, services=services)
    44→    else:
    45→        print(colorize(f"  Unknown runner: {runner}. Use 'codex' or 'claude'.", "red"))
    46→
    47→
    48→__all__ = ["_parse_only_stages", "do_run_triage_stages"]
    49→
```

```
    44→
    45→def _auto_confirm_stage(
    46→    *,
    47→    plan: dict,
    48→    stage_record: dict,
    49→    stage_name: str,
    50→    stage_label: str,
    51→    attestation: str | None,
    52→    blocked_heading: str,
    53→    confirm_cmd: str,
    54→    inline_hint: str,
    55→    dimensions: list[str] | None = None,
    56→    cluster_names: list[str] | None = None,
    57→    save_plan_fn=None,
    58→) -> bool:
    59→    """Shared auto-confirm flow for stage fold-confirm operations."""
    60→    if save_plan_fn is None:
    61→        save_plan_fn = save_plan
    62→    if stage_record.get("confirmed_at"):
    63→        return True
    64→    if not attestation or len(attestation.strip()) < _MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN:
    65→        print(colorize(f"  {blocked_heading}", "red"))
    66→        print(colorize(f"  Run: {confirm_cmd}", "dim"))
    67→        print(colorize(f"  {inline_hint}", "dim"))
    68→        return False
    69→
    70→    confirmed_text = attestation.strip()
    71→    validation_err = _validate_attestation(
    72→        confirmed_text,
    73→        stage_name,
    74→        dimensions=dimensions,
    75→        cluster_names=cluster_names,
    76→    )
    77→    if validation_err:
    78→        print(colorize(f"  {validation_err}", "red"))
    79→        return False
    80→
    81→    stage_record["confirmed_at"] = utc_now()
    82→    stage_record["confirmed_text"] = confirmed_text
    83→    save_plan_fn(plan)
    84→    print(colorize(f"  ✓ {stage_label} auto-confirmed via --attestation.", "green"))
    85→    return True
    86→
    87→
    88→def _auto_confirm_observe_if_attested(
    89→    *,
    90→    plan: dict,
    91→    stages: dict,
    92→    attestation: str | None,
    93→    triage_input,
    94→    save_plan_fn=None,
    95→) -> bool:
    96→    observe_stage = stages.get("observe")
    97→    if observe_stage is None:
    98→        return False
    99→    _by_dim, dim_names = observe_dimension_breakdown(triage_input)
   100→    return _auto_confirm_stage(
   101→        plan=plan,
   102→        stage_record=observe_stage,
   103→        stage_name="observe",
   104→        stage_label="Observe",
   105→        attestation=attestation,
   106→        blocked_heading="Cannot reflect: observe stage not confirmed.",
   107→        confirm_cmd="desloppify plan triage --confirm observe",
   108→        inline_hint="Or pass --attestation to auto-confirm observe inline.",
   109→        dimensions=dim_names,
   110→        save_plan_fn=save_plan_fn,
   111→    )
   112→
   113→
   114→def _validate_recurring_dimension_mentions(
   115→    *,
   116→    report: str,
   117→    recurring_dims: list[str],
   118→    recurring: dict,
   119→) -> bool:
   120→    if not recurring_dims:
   121→        return True
   122→    report_lower = report.lower()
   123→    mentioned = [dim for dim in recurring_dims if dim.lower() in report_lower]
   124→    if mentioned:
   125→        return True
   126→    print(colorize("  Recurring patterns detected but not addressed in report:", "red"))
   127→    for dim in recurring_dims:
   128→        info = recurring[dim]
   129→        print(
   130→            colorize(
   131→                f"    {dim}: {len(info['resolved'])} resolved, "
   132→                f"{len(info['open'])} still open — potential loop",
   133→                "yellow",
   134→            )
   135→        )
   136→    print(colorize("  Your report must mention at least one recurring dimension name.", "dim"))
   137→    return False
   138→
   139→
   140→def _require_reflect_stage_for_organize(stages: dict) -> bool:
   141→    if "reflect" in stages:
   142→        return True
   143→    if "observe" not in stages:
   144→        print(colorize("  Cannot organize: observe stage not complete.", "red"))
   145→        print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "..."', "dim"))
   146→        return False
   147→    print(colorize("  Cannot organize: reflect stage not complete.", "red"))
   148→    print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "..."', "dim"))
   149→    return False
   150→
   151→
   152→def _auto_confirm_reflect_for_organize(
   153→    *,
   154→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   155→    plan: dict,
   156→    stages: dict,
   157→    attestation: str | None,
   158→    triage_input=None,
   159→    command_runtime_fn=None,
   160→    collect_triage_input_fn=collect_triage_input,
   161→    detect_recurring_patterns_fn=detect_recurring_patterns,
   162→    save_plan_fn=None,
   163→) -> bool:
   164→    reflect_stage = stages.get("reflect")
   165→    if reflect_stage is None:
   166→        return False
   167→
   168→    resolved_triage_input = triage_input
   169→    if resolved_triage_input is None:
   170→        runtime_factory = command_runtime_fn or command_runtime
   171→        runtime = runtime_factory(args)
   172→        resolved_triage_input = collect_triage_input_fn(plan, runtime.state)
   173→
   174→    recurring = detect_recurring_patterns_fn(
   175→        resolved_triage_input.open_issues,
   176→        resolved_triage_input.resolved_issues,
   177→    )
   178→    _by_dim, observe_dims = observe_dimension_breakdown(resolved_triage_input)
   179→    reflect_dims = sorted(set((list(recurring.keys()) if recurring else []) + observe_dims))
   180→    reflect_clusters = [name for name in plan.get("clusters", {}) if not plan["clusters"][name].get("auto")]
   181→    return _auto_confirm_stage(
   182→        plan=plan,
   183→        stage_record=reflect_stage,
   184→        stage_name="reflect",
   185→        stage_label="Reflect",
   186→        attestation=attestation,
   187→        blocked_heading="Cannot organize: reflect stage not confirmed.",
   188→        confirm_cmd="desloppify plan triage --confirm reflect",
   189→        inline_hint="Or pass --attestation to auto-confirm reflect inline.",
   190→        dimensions=reflect_dims,
   191→        cluster_names=reflect_clusters,
   192→        save_plan_fn=save_plan_fn,
   193→    )
   194→
   195→
   196→def _manual_clusters_or_error(plan: dict) -> list[str] | None:
   197→    manual_clusters = manual_clusters_with_issues(plan)
   198→    if manual_clusters:
   199→        return manual_clusters
   200→    any_clusters = [name for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items() if cluster.get("issue_ids")]
   201→    if any_clusters:
   202→        print(colorize("  Cannot organize: only auto-clusters exist.", "red"))
   203→        print(colorize("  Create manual clusters that group issues by root cause:", "dim"))
   204→    else:
   205→        print(colorize("  Cannot organize: no clusters with issues exist.", "red"))
   206→    print(colorize('    desloppify plan cluster create <name> --description "..."', "dim"))
   207→    print(colorize("    desloppify plan cluster add <name> <issue-patterns>", "dim"))
   208→    return None
   209→
   210→
   211→def _clusters_enriched_or_error(plan: dict) -> bool:
   212→    gaps = unenriched_clusters(plan)
   213→    if not gaps:
   214→        return True
   215→    print(colorize(f"  Cannot organize: {len(gaps)} cluster(s) need enrichment.", "red"))
   216→    for name, missing in gaps:
   217→        print(colorize(f"    {name}: missing {', '.join(missing)}", "yellow"))
   218→    print()
   219→    print(colorize("  Each cluster needs a description and action steps:", "dim"))
   220→    print(colorize('    desloppify plan cluster update <name> --description "what this cluster addresses" --steps "step 1" "step 2"', "dim"))
   221→    return False
   222→
   223→
   224→def _unclustered_review_issues_or_error(plan: dict, state: dict) -> bool:
   225→    """Block if open review issues aren't in any manual cluster. Return True if OK."""
   226→    unclustered = unclustered_review_issues(plan, state)
   227→    if not unclustered:
   228→        return True
   229→    print(colorize(f"  Cannot organize: {len(unclustered)} review issue(s) have no cluster.", "red"))
   230→    for fid in unclustered[:10]:
   231→        short = fid.rsplit("::", 2)[-2] if "::" in fid else fid
   232→        print(colorize(f"    {short}", "yellow"))
   233→    if len(unclustered) > 10:
   234→        print(colorize(f"    ... and {len(unclustered) - 10} more", "yellow"))
   235→    print()
   236→    print(colorize("  Every review issue needs an action plan. Either:", "dim"))
   237→    print(colorize("    1. Add to a cluster: desloppify plan cluster add <name> <pattern>", "dim"))
   238→    print(colorize('    2. Wontfix it: desloppify plan skip --permanent <pattern> --note "reason" --attest "..."', "dim"))
   239→    return False
   240→
   241→
   242→def _organize_report_or_error(report: str | None) -> str | None:
   243→    if not report:
   244→        print(colorize("  --report is required for --stage organize.", "red"))
   245→        print(colorize("  Summarize your prioritized organization:", "dim"))
   246→        print(colorize("  - Did you defer contradictory issues before clustering?", "dim"))
   247→        print(colorize("  - What clusters did you create and why?", "dim"))
   248→        print(colorize("  - Explicit priority ordering: which cluster 1st, 2nd, 3rd and why?", "dim"))
   249→        print(colorize("  - What depends on what? What unblocks the most?", "dim"))
   250→        return None
   251→    if len(report) < 100:
   252→        print(colorize(f"  Report too short: {len(report)} chars (minimum 100).", "red"))
   253→        print(colorize("  Explain what you organized, your priorities, and focus order.", "dim"))
   254→        return None
   255→    return report
   256→
   257→
   258→__all__ = [
   259→    "_auto_confirm_enrich_for_complete",
   260→    "_auto_confirm_observe_if_attested",
   261→    "_auto_confirm_organize_for_complete",
   262→    "_auto_confirm_reflect_for_organize",
   263→    "_cluster_file_overlaps",
   264→    "_clusters_with_directory_scatter",
   265→    "_clusters_with_high_step_ratio",
   266→    "_clusters_enriched_or_error",
   267→    "_enrich_report_or_error",
   268→    "_unclustered_review_issues_or_error",
   269→    "_completion_clusters_valid",
   270→    "_completion_strategy_valid",
   271→    "_confirm_existing_stages_valid",
   272→    "_confirm_note_valid",
```

```
    11→
    12→def _resolve_state_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    13→    return path if path is not None else state_mod.get_state_file()
    14→
    15→
    16→def _resolve_plan_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    17→    return path if path is not None else get_plan_file()
    18→
    19→
    20→def _plan_file_for_state(state_file: Path | None) -> Path | None:
    21→    if state_file is None:
    22→        return None
    23→    return plan_path_for_state(state_file)
    24→
    25→
    26→def _snapshot_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    27→    if not path.exists():
    28→        return None
    29→    return path.read_text()
    30→
    31→
    32→def _restore_file_snapshot(path: Path, snapshot: str | None) -> None:
    33→    if snapshot is None:
    34→        try:
    35→            path.unlink()
    36→        except FileNotFoundError:
    37→            return
    38→        return
    39→    safe_write_text(path, snapshot)
    40→
    41→
    42→def save_plan_state_transactional(
    43→    *,
    44→    plan: dict,
    45→    plan_path: Path | None,
    46→    state_data: dict,
    47→    state_path_value: Path | None,
    48→) -> None:
    49→    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
    50→    effective_plan_path = _resolve_plan_file(plan_path)
    51→    effective_state_path = _resolve_state_file(state_path_value)
    52→    plan_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_plan_path)
    53→    state_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_state_path)
    54→
    55→    try:
    56→        state_mod.save_state(state_data, effective_state_path)
    57→        # Keep this import-time indirection so tests monkeypatching
    58→        # override_handlers.save_plan still affect transactional writes.
    59→        from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    60→
    61→        host.save_plan(plan, effective_plan_path)
    62→    except Exception:
    63→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_state_path, state_snapshot)
    64→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_plan_path, plan_snapshot)
    65→        raise
    66→
    67→
    68→__all__ = [
    69→    "_plan_file_for_state",
    70→    "save_plan_state_transactional",
    71→]
    72→
```

```
   148→    """Check or skip the scan requirement for workflow items."""
   149→    from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
   150→
   151→    skip = getattr(args, "skip", False)
   152→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
   153→

   159→        print(colorize("  Scan gate is not applicable — workflow items gate themselves.", "dim"))
   160→        return

   163→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(resolved_state_path)
   164→    current_scan_count = int(state_data.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   165→    scan_ran = current_scan_count > scan_count_at_start
   166→    scan_skipped = plan.get("scan_gate_skipped", False)
   167→
   168→    if not skip:
   169→        if scan_ran:
   170→            print(colorize("  Scan gate: PASSED", "green"))
   171→            print(colorize(f"  Scans at cycle start: {scan_count_at_start}  Current: {current_scan_count}", "dim"))
   172→        elif scan_skipped:

   174→        else:
   175→            print(colorize("  Scan gate: BLOCKED", "red"))
   176→            print(colorize(f"  Scans at cycle start: {scan_count_at_start}  Current: {current_scan_count}", "dim"))
   177→            print(colorize("  Run: desloppify scan", "dim"))
   178→            print(colorize('  Or:  desloppify plan scan-gate --skip --note "reason"', "dim"))
   179→        return

   181→    if scan_ran:

   183→        return
   184→
   185→    if not note or len(note.strip()) < 50:

   187→        return
   188→
   189→    plan["scan_gate_skipped"] = True
   190→    host.append_log_entry(

   194→        note=note,
```

```
1→"""Merge-support scoring and issue-key helpers for batch review results."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import cast
     6→
     7→from desloppify.intelligence.review.feedback_contract import (
     8→    LEGACY_REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
     9→    REVIEW_QUALITY_HIGH_SCORE_MISSING_ISSUES_KEY,
    10→)
    11→from desloppify.intelligence.review.issue_merge import (
    12→    merge_list_fields,
    13→    normalize_word_set,
    14→    pick_longer_text,
    15→    track_merged_from,
    16→)
    17→
    18→from .core_models import (
    19→    BatchDimensionNotePayload,
    20→    BatchIssuePayload,
    21→    BatchResultPayload,
    22→)
    23→from .scoring import DimensionMergeScorer
    24→
    25→_DIMENSION_SCORER = DimensionMergeScorer()
    26→
    27→
    28→def assessment_weight(
    29→    *,
    30→    dimension: str,
    31→    issues: list[BatchIssuePayload],
    32→    dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload],
    33→) -> float:
    34→    """Evidence-weighted assessment score weight with a neutral floor.
    35→
    36→    Weighting is evidence-based and score-independent: the raw score does not
    37→    influence how much weight a batch contributes during merge.
    38→    """
    39→    note = dimension_notes.get(dimension, {})
    40→    note_evidence = len(note.get("evidence", [])) if isinstance(note, dict) else 0
    41→    issue_count = sum(
    42→        1
    43→        for issue in issues
    44→        if str(issue.get("dimension", "")).strip() == dimension
    45→    )
    46→    return float(1 + note_evidence + issue_count)
    47→
    48→
    49→def _issue_pressure_by_dimension(
    50→    issues: list[BatchIssuePayload],
    51→    *,
    52→    dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload],
    53→) -> tuple[dict[str, float], dict[str, int]]:
    54→    """Summarize how strongly issues should pull dimension scores down."""
    55→    return _DIMENSION_SCORER.issue_pressure_by_dimension(
    56→        issues,
    57→        dimension_notes=dimension_notes,
    58→    )
    59→
    60→
    61→def _accumulate_batch_scores(
    62→    result: BatchResultPayload,
    63→    *,
    64→    score_buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]],
    65→    score_raw_by_dim: dict[str, list[float]],
    66→    merged_dimension_notes: dict[str, BatchDimensionNotePayload],
    67→    abstraction_axis_scores: dict[str, list[tuple[float, float]]],
    68→    abstraction_sub_axes: tuple[str, ...],
    69→) -> None:
    70→    """Accumulate assessment scores, dimension notes, and sub-axis data from one batch."""
    71→    result_issues = result.get("issues", [])
    72→    result_notes = result.get("dimension_notes", {})
    73→    for key, score in result.get("assessments", {}).items():
    74→        if isinstance(score, bool):
    75→            continue
    76→        score_value = float(score)
    77→        weight = assessment_weight(
    78→            dimension=key,
    79→            issues=result_issues,
    80→            dimension_notes=result_notes,
```

```
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize

    10→_PATH_RE = re.compile(r"(?:src|supabase)/[\w./-]+\.\w+")
    11→_EXT_SWAPS = {".ts": ".tsx", ".tsx": ".ts", ".js": ".jsx", ".jsx": ".js"}

    14→
    15→def _require_organize_stage_for_enrich(stages: dict) -> bool:
    16→    """Gate: organize must be done before enrich."""
    17→    if "organize" in stages:
    18→        return True
    19→    if "observe" not in stages:
    20→        print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: observe stage not complete.", "red"))
    21→        print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "..."', "dim"))
    22→        return False
    23→    if "reflect" not in stages:
    24→        print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: reflect stage not complete.", "red"))
    25→        print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "..."', "dim"))
    26→        return False
    27→    print(colorize("  Cannot enrich: organize stage not complete.", "red"))
    28→    print(colorize('  Run: desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "..."', "dim"))
    29→    return False
    30→
    31→
    32→def _underspecified_steps(plan: dict) -> list[tuple[str, int, int]]:
    33→    """Return (cluster_name, bare_count, total_count) for steps missing detail or issue_refs."""
    34→    results: list[tuple[str, int, int]] = []
    35→    for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    36→        if cluster.get("auto") or not cluster.get("issue_ids"):
    37→            continue
    38→        steps = cluster.get("action_steps") or []
    39→        if not steps:
    40→            continue
    41→        bare = sum(
    42→            1
    43→            for step in steps
    44→            if isinstance(step, dict) and (not step.get("detail") or not step.get("issue_refs"))
    45→        )
    46→        if bare > 0:
    47→            results.append((name, bare, len(steps)))
    48→    return results
    49→
    50→
```

## Analysis Report: Module Split Patterns

Based on my thorough examination of the codebase, here's the fundamental pattern of module splits on this branch:

---

### 1. **override_handlers.py** — THIN SHELL (re-export/dispatch pattern)

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py`

**Size:** 141 lines

**Structure:**
- **Imports:** Lines 1-62 (62 lines of imports, constants, logger)
- **Logic:** Lines 63-130 (only 11 trivial 1-3 line functions)
- **Exports:** Lines 132-141 (__all__ list)

**Actual Code Pattern:**
```python
def _save_plan_state_transactional(...) -> None:
    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
    _save_plan_state_transactional_impl(...)  # Pure delegation

def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    _cmd_plan_describe_impl(args)  # Pure delegation
```

**Verdict:** **Pure thin shell.** Every function is a 1-line delegation wrapper that forwards to a `_*_impl` function in split-out modules (`override_io`, `override_misc`, `override_resolve_cmd`, `override_skip`, etc.). The hub file contains zero orchestration logic—it's purely a re-export facade. No imports are avoided by going through it.

**Split modules total: 1003 lines across 6 files**

---

### 2. **cluster_handlers.py** — THIN SHELL (dispatcher + helper functions)

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cluster_handlers.py`

**Size:** 218 lines

**Structure:**
- **Imports:** Lines 1-33 (33 lines)
- **Logic:** Lines 35-215 (180 lines)
  - Helper functions: `_suggest_close_matches()` (38 lines), `_print_pattern_hints()` (9 lines)
  - Subcommand handlers: `_cmd_cluster_add()` (52 lines), `_cmd_cluster_remove()` (32 lines), `_cmd_cluster_update()` (14 lines)
  - Main dispatcher: `cmd_cluster_dispatch()` (19 lines)

**Actual Code Pattern:**
- **Routing logic:** The `cmd_cluster_dispatch()` function is a simple dictionary-based router that maps action names to imported handlers:
```python
def cmd_cluster_dispatch(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    cluster_action = getattr(args, "cluster_action", None)
    dispatch = {
        "create": _cmd_cluster_create,
        "add": _cmd_cluster_add,
        "remove": _cmd_cluster_remove,
        ...
    }
    handler = dispatch.get(cluster_action)
    if handler is None:
        _cmd_cluster_list(args)
    handler(args)
```

- **Thin wrappers:** `_cmd_cluster_add()` and `_cmd_cluster_remove()` contain orchestration logic (input validation, plan loading, pattern resolution, dry-run handling, logging) but still delegate substantive operations to `engine.plan` module functions.

- **Helper functions:** `_suggest_close_matches()` and `_print_pattern_hints()` contain non-trivial logic specific to cluster command UX (fuzzy matching, suggestions).

**Verdict:** **Thin dispatcher with light helper logic.** The hub performs routing and argument validation, but delegates actual mutations to split modules. The helper functions could be in a separate file but are kept here for tight coupling to UX concerns.

**Split modules total: 1146 lines across 6 files** (cluster_ops_manage, cluster_ops_display, cluster_ops_reorder, cluster_update, cluster_steps)

---

### 3. **triage/runner/orchestrator.py** — THIN DISPATCHER

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/runner/orchestrator.py`

**Size:** 49 lines

**Structure:**
- **Imports:** Lines 1-12 (12 lines)
- **Logic:** Lines 15-46 (31 lines)
  - Helper: `_parse_only_stages()` (8 lines of validation)
  - Main: `do_run_triage_stages()` (18 lines of mode selection)

**Actual Code Pattern:**
```python
def do_run_triage_stages(args, *, services=None) -> None:
    runner = str(getattr(args, "runner", "codex")).strip().lower()
    try:
        stages_to_run = _parse_only_stages(getattr(args, "only_stages", None))
    except ValueError as exc:
        print(colorize(f"  {exc}", "red"))
        return
    
    if runner == "claude":
        run_claude_orchestrator(args, services=services)
    elif runner == "codex":
        run_codex_pipeline(args, stages_to_run=stages_to_run, services=services)
    else:
        print(colorize(f"  Unknown runner: {runner}. Use 'codex' or 'claude'.", "red"))
```

**Split modules:**
- `orchestrator_claude.py` (48 lines) — prints instructions for Claude mode
- `orchestrator_codex_pipeline.py` (293 lines) — full codex pipeline implementation
- `orchestrator_codex_observe.py` (136 lines) — observe stage logic
- `orchestrator_codex_sense.py` (135 lines) — sense-check stage logic
- `orchestrator_common.py` (27 lines) — constants (STAGES tuple) and helpers

**Verdict:** **Pure thin dispatcher.** The orchestrator.py file contains no real logic—just mode selection and argument parsing. All actual work happens in split modules. This is the simplest pattern: the hub is a command-level routing point.

**Split modules total: 639 lines across 5 files**

### 4. **review/batch/core.py** — ULTRA-THIN RE-EXPORT FACADE

**Size:** 60 lines

**Structure:**
- **Imports:** Lines 1-17 (17 lines)

**Actual Code Pattern:**
```python
def merge_batch_results(batch_results, *, abstraction_sub_axes, abstraction_component_names):
    from .merge import merge_batch_results as _merge_batch_results
    return _merge_batch_results(
        batch_results,
        abstraction_sub_axes=abstraction_sub_axes,
        abstraction_component_names=abstraction_component_names,
    )

def build_batch_prompt(*, repo_root, packet_path, batch_index, batch):

```

**Split modules:**
- `core_models.py` (104 lines) — TypedDicts and dataclasses (BatchIssuePayload, BatchDimensionNotePayload, BatchResultPayload, NormalizedBatchIssue)
- `core_parse.py` (59 lines) — JSON extraction and batch selection parsing
- `core_normalize.py` (416 lines) — normalization logic
- `core_merge_support.py` (234 lines) — merge scoring and issue-key helpers

**Verdict:** **The thinnest shell.** Pure re-export facade. No logic at all—each function is a straight pass-through. This is a backwards-compatibility layer that re-imports from split modules and re-exports them. The file exists only to avoid breaking existing import paths (e.g., `from .core import merge_batch_results` instead of `from .merge import merge_batch_results`).

**Split modules total: 813 lines across 4 files**

### 5. **_stage_validation.py** — MIXED (validator orchestrator + re-exports)

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/triage/_stage_validation.py`

**Size:** 293 lines

**Structure:**
- **Imports:** Lines 1-42 (42 lines of imports from split modules)
- **Logic:** Lines 45-256 (211 lines of actual validator functions)
- **Exports:** Lines 258-293 (36 items in __all__)

**Actual Code Pattern:**
```python
def _auto_confirm_stage(
    *,
    plan: dict,
    stage_record: dict,
    stage_name: str,
    stage_label: str,
    attestation: str | None,
    ...,
) -> bool:
    """Shared auto-confirm flow for stage fold-confirm operations."""
    if save_plan_fn is None:
        save_plan_fn = save_plan
    if stage_record.get("confirmed_at"):
        return True
    if not attestation or len(attestation.strip()) < _MIN_ATTESTATION_LEN:
        # ... prints error messages
        return False
    
    confirmed_text = attestation.strip()
    validation_err = _validate_attestation(...)
    if validation_err:
        # ... prints error
        return False
    
    stage_record["confirmed_at"] = utc_now()
    # ... mutates plan
    return True
```

**Verdict:** **Orchestrator + facade hybrid.** This file contains genuine orchestration logic (12+ real functions with conditional branching, validation, error messages). BUT it also imports from 3-4 split modules and re-exports them in its __all__ list, acting as a facade. The split modules handle specialized concerns:

- `_stage_validation_completion_policy.py` (179 lines) — completion strategy/policy validation
- `_stage_validation_completion_stages.py` (189 lines) — auto-confirm stage helpers
- `_stage_validation_enrich_checks.py` (247 lines) — enrichment and cluster-level validation

**The pattern here is deliberate:** The hub file (_stage_validation.py) is the orchestrator for validation flows, while split files handle domain-specific validation rules. This is more complex than the pure facades but still primarily dispatcher/router.

**Split modules total: 908 lines across 4 files**

## Summary Table

| File | Size | Pattern | Real Logic | Delegation |
|------|------|---------|-----------|-----------|
| `override_handlers.py` | 141 | Pure thin shell | 11 x 1-line wrappers (0 logic) | 100% to split modules |
| `cluster_handlers.py` | 218 | Dispatcher + helpers | 2 helpers + routing (180 lines) | Mutations to split modules |
| `orchestrator.py` | 49 | Pure dispatcher | Mode selection + validation (30 lines) | 100% to split modules |
| `core.py` (batch) | 60 | Re-export facade | 2 x 1-line pass-throughs (0 logic) | 100% to split modules |
| `_stage_validation.py` | 293 | Validator orchestrator | 12+ real validators (211 lines) | Rule checks to split modules |

## Fundamental Pattern

**All five cases follow the same split principle:**

1. **The hub file is a "thin shell"** — either:
   - Pure re-export facade (core.py, override_handlers.py) — just re-imports and re-exports
   - Dispatcher (orchestrator.py, cluster_handlers.py) — routes to implementation modules
   - Orchestrator (\_stage_validation.py) — coordinates high-level flow and re-exports

2. **Split modules hold the real logic:**
   - Overrides → split into `override_io`, `override_misc`, `override_skip`, etc. (domain-specific operations)
   - Cluster ops → split into `cluster_ops_manage`, `cluster_ops_display`, `cluster_ops_reorder` (operation type-based)
   - Orchestrators → split into `orchestrator_claude`, `orchestrator_codex_pipeline`, etc. (runner/mode-based)
   - Batch core → split into `core_models`, `core_parse`, `core_normalize`, `core_merge_support` (concern-based)
   - Stage validation → split into `*_completion_policy`, `*_completion_stages`, `*_enrich_checks` (domain-based)

3. **No logic is duplicated** — each split module is the single source of truth for its domain.

4. **Import paths are often preserved** — the hub files sometimes act as backwards-compat facades (esp. core.py, _stage_validation.py) to avoid breaking external consumers, but new code imports directly from split modules.

Perfect. Now let me create a comprehensive summary document based on my findings:

## Summary: Analysis of Module Splits on `wip/triage-runner` Branch

Based on my investigation of the git history and file sizes, here's what's driving these splits:

### 1. **Scale of the Refactoring**
The branch contains **30+ split commits**, systematically breaking down large modules across three command packages:
- Plan command (override_handlers, cluster_handlers, stage_validation)
- Triage runner (orchestrator, validation, confirmation handlers, etc.)
- Review command (batch core)
- Plus TypeScript detector modules and shared infrastructure

### 2. **Original File Sizes (Before Splits)**

| File | Original Size | Status |
|------|--------------|--------|
| `override_handlers.py` | 880 lines | Split into 5 focused modules |
| `cluster_handlers.py` | 879 lines | Split into 6 focused modules |
| `_stage_validation.py` | 463 lines | Split into 4 focused modules |
| `orchestrator.py` | 687 lines (before split) | Split into 5 focused modules |
| `batch/core.py` | 720 lines | Split into 4 focused modules |

**All original files were between 460-880 lines** — genuinely large, monolithic modules.

### 3. **Post-Split Outcomes**

| Split Group | Original | After Split | Module Count |
|------------|----------|-------------|--------------|
| Override handlers | 880 | 1,144 total | 5 modules |
| Cluster handlers | 879 | 1,364 total | 6 modules |
| Stage validation | 463 | 908 total | 4 modules |
| Orchestrator | 687 | 687 total | 5 modules |
| Batch core | 720 | 872 total | 4 modules |

**The line counts stayed roughly flat or slightly increased** (due to added imports and module boundaries), but the responsibility is now distributed across focused, single-purpose modules.

### 4. **What Desloppify Reports**

Running `python -m desloppify --lang python scan --path desloppify` shows:
- **Score: 75.1/100** (target: 95.0)
- **Test health collapsed**: 97.9% → 80.6% (-17.3%)
- **Code quality: 96.4%** (still solid)
- **Key drags**: Low elegance, type safety, design coherence

The scan does **NOT** flag file size as a primary issue. The mechanical detectors (unused code, complexity, coupling) show mostly healthy results. Instead, the subjective review dimensions (elegance, design coherence, type safety) are what's struggling.

### 5. **The Real Pattern**

These splits appear **intentional and strategic** rather than mechanical:

1. **Before the split**: Large files with mixed concerns (I/O, validation logic, orchestration, transformation)
2. **After the split**: Focused modules with clear responsibilities:
   - `override_io.py` — serialization/deserialization
   - `override_resolve_cmd.py` — resolve-command handling
   - `override_skip.py` — skip-item logic
   - etc.

3. **The timing**: These splits are happening on a **refactoring branch** (`wip/triage-runner`), not merged to main. The commit messages use "refactor: split" consistently, suggesting this is phase 2 of a larger cleanup effort.

4. **Not driven by desloppify**: The tool is flagging subjective issues (elegance, design, type safety) but not screaming about file size. The splits appear to be **intentional architectural choices** made during a broader refactoring campaign.

### Conclusion

**The splits are solving real problems**: large files with multiple responsibilities are being decomposed into focused modules. However, the **tool itself (desloppify) isn't recommending these splits** — it's more concerned about cross-cutting subjective qualities (elegance, design coherence, type safety). This is a **purposeful refactoring effort** (likely improving testability and separation of concerns) rather than a mechanical response to desloppify findings.

```
1→"""Triage stage pipeline entrypoint — Codex subprocess runner and Claude orchestrator."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     8→
     9→from ..services import TriageServices
    10→from .orchestrator_claude import run_claude_orchestrator
    11→from .orchestrator_codex_pipeline import run_codex_pipeline
    12→from .orchestrator_common import STAGES
    13→
    14→
    15→def _parse_only_stages(raw: str | None) -> list[str]:
    16→    """Parse --only-stages comma-separated string into validated stage list."""
    17→    if not raw:
    18→        return list(STAGES)
    19→    stages = [s.strip().lower() for s in raw.split(",") if s.strip()]
    20→    for stage in stages:
    21→        if stage not in STAGES:
    22→            raise ValueError(f"Unknown stage: {stage!r}. Valid: {', '.join(STAGES)}")
    23→    return stages
    24→
    25→
    26→def do_run_triage_stages(
    27→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    28→    *,
    29→    services: TriageServices | None = None,
    30→) -> None:
    31→    """Run triage stages via the selected runner."""
    32→    runner = str(getattr(args, "runner", "codex")).strip().lower()
    33→
    34→    try:
    35→        stages_to_run = _parse_only_stages(getattr(args, "only_stages", None))
    36→    except ValueError as exc:
    37→        print(colorize(f"  {exc}", "red"))
    38→        return
    39→
    40→    if runner == "claude":
    41→        run_claude_orchestrator(args, services=services)
    42→    elif runner == "codex":
    43→        run_codex_pipeline(args, stages_to_run=stages_to_run, services=services)
    44→    else:
    45→        print(colorize(f"  Unknown runner: {runner}. Use 'codex' or 'claude'.", "red"))
    46→
    47→
    48→__all__ = ["_parse_only_stages", "do_run_triage_stages"]
    49→
```

```
1→"""I/O and transactional persistence helpers for plan override commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.base.discovery.file_paths import safe_write_text
     9→from desloppify.engine.plan import get_plan_file, plan_path_for_state
    10→
    11→
    12→def _resolve_state_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    13→    return path if path is not None else state_mod.get_state_file()
    14→
    15→
    16→def _resolve_plan_file(path: Path | None) -> Path:
    17→    return path if path is not None else get_plan_file()
    18→
    19→
    20→def _plan_file_for_state(state_file: Path | None) -> Path | None:
    21→    if state_file is None:
    22→        return None
    23→    return plan_path_for_state(state_file)
    24→
    25→
    26→def _snapshot_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    27→    if not path.exists():
    28→        return None
    29→    return path.read_text()
    30→
    31→
    32→def _restore_file_snapshot(path: Path, snapshot: str | None) -> None:
    33→    if snapshot is None:
    34→        try:
    35→            path.unlink()
    36→        except FileNotFoundError:
    37→            return
    38→        return
    39→    safe_write_text(path, snapshot)
    40→
    41→
    42→def save_plan_state_transactional(
    43→    *,
    44→    plan: dict,
    45→    plan_path: Path | None,
    46→    state_data: dict,
    47→    state_path_value: Path | None,
    48→) -> None:
    49→    """Persist plan+state together; rollback both files on partial write failure."""
    50→    effective_plan_path = _resolve_plan_file(plan_path)
    51→    effective_state_path = _resolve_state_file(state_path_value)
    52→    plan_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_plan_path)
    53→    state_snapshot = _snapshot_file(effective_state_path)
    54→
    55→    try:
    56→        state_mod.save_state(state_data, effective_state_path)
    57→        # Keep this import-time indirection so tests monkeypatching
    58→        # override_handlers.save_plan still affect transactional writes.
    59→        from . import override_handlers as host  # noqa: PLC0415
    60→
    61→        host.save_plan(plan, effective_plan_path)
    62→    except Exception:
    63→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_state_path, state_snapshot)
    64→        _restore_file_snapshot(effective_plan_path, plan_snapshot)
    65→        raise
    66→
    67→
    68→__all__ = [
    69→    "_plan_file_for_state",
    70→    "save_plan_state_transactional",
    71→]
    72→
```
